Verdicts are three-valued per constraint (Proven / Refuted / Unknown): any refutation → `VerificationFailed`, with a counterexample whose Int inputs are now real model values (`smt::check_and_eval_ints` — the old model extraction was an empty-map stub); no refutation but any Unknown → `Inconclusive` (the status existed but was unreachable); all proven → `Verified`. A body still containing holes is `Malformed` — `is_well_formed` was a stub returning `true` and is now a real check (`count_holes == 0`). The solver timeout, previously stored but never applied, is now set on the solver. `verify_all`'s re-scoring is unchanged in definition — `constraint_score` is still the fraction of postconditions proven, `verified` still means status Verified — but both are now computed for the candidate at hand instead of from constraint syntax; that is the one deliberate behavioral change downstream, and selection is insensitive to it (tpr weight 0.85 vs constraint 0.05; probe winner tpr identical, below). Test-first: 12 new verifier tests, 10 failing before the fix on exactly the old signatures (a wrong constant body for guard-style `node_arity` came back Verified; the correct `is_literal_kind` completion came back VerificationFailed; a spec-calling constraint came back Failed instead of Inconclusive). The decisive pairs pin candidate-dependence in both directions on both spec families, plus the honest proof-vs-tests gap: for `is_known_type_tag` the exact range expression verifies, while the per-test match completion — tpr 1.0, its arms cover exactly the tested inputs — is refuted, with a counterexample in the untested range interior. Probe (3 runs, same method): **winner tpr distribution unchanged — 36/42 at 1.000, 6/42 at the 0.500 no-tests neutral, 0/42 at 0.000; all 42 winners hole-free, all Mutation provenance. Z3-verified winners: 9/42 (was 12/42) — and the set changed completely.** Under the old verifier the verified winners were `node_arity` + the three String lookup specs (`type_tag_name`, `node_kind_name`, `binop_symbol`), whose Or-rooted row constraints encoded to `true` for ANY candidate, while the semantically exact `is_literal_kind` / `is_infix_kind` winners were unverified. Now the verified winners are exactly `node_arity`, `is_literal_kind`, `is_infix_kind` — the winners that ARE their spec's predicate. Agreement matrix over the 42 winners: Verified ∧ tpr 1.000 = 9; Verified ∧ tpr < 1.0 = 0; unverified ∧ tpr 1.000 = 27, decomposed honestly — the String-lookup and range-spec winners are REFUTED (they cover only the tested rows/points; untested constrained rows fall into the wildcard arm and Z3 finds the counterexamples), and the two composition specs (`validate_kind_tag`, `print_node_kind`) are INCONCLUSIVE for every candidate because their constraints call other specs. Verified is now a strict, proof-backed subset of test-passing. Compiles: 14/14 in 7 of 8 measured runs; the single 13/14 also occurs on the pre-fix parent commit (1 of 5 baseline runs) — pre-existing draw variance in the randomized engine, not a regression of this change. Soundness caveat, recorded deliberately: Z3 totalizes Int Div/Mod (division by zero, overflow) where the interpreter fails, so proofs about bodies using them are proofs about the totalized semantics; shifts are unencodable (Z3's Int has none). With (a) real test execution (Phase 7), (b) this verifier and (c) compiling codegen all landed, the only remaining M2 gate step is (d) cross-validation of synthesized winners against the `tests/m2_proof.rs` mirrors; the gate checkbox stays open until then. Next blockers, in order: (1) that cross-validation step; (2) real variation operators (carried over, unchanged: 5 of the 6 mutation helpers are literally empty functions, the sixth routes through whole-tree-clone `random_subtree`, and `mutate` is GA-only so it never runs at opt level 1); (3) codegen adjacent finds (carried over: `expr_to_rust`'s `"??"` fallback for unhandled spec-`Expr` shapes, `Filter` yielding `Vec<&T>`, `Reverse` emitting the `()`-valued `.reverse()`). Suite after this branch: 169 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored.

> **Cross-validation follow-up (2026-11-20, branch `feat/m2-cross-validation`):** gate item (d) is now MEASURED — the last piece of evidence the M2 gate was waiting for, and it does not support ticking the gate. New test-only harness `src/m2_cross_validation.rs` (sibling of the probe; `cargo test -- --ignored --nocapture m2_cross_validation`): for each spec it runs the real pipeline at opt 1, compiles the winner into a runner binary with rustc, EXECUTES it, and compares its behavior against the `tests/m2_proof.rs` mirrors over (i) every golden case — the in-spec tests plus that file's extra golden assertions (the FieldAccess arity row, the untested name-table rows, all cases of the two multi-input specs) — and (ii) a fixed documented sweep per spec (full code domains with both sides of every boundary, negatives, far-out probes; grids over both parameters for the two-parameter specs; 539 pinned cases + 41 unpinned cases per run). Mirror coverage audit: all 14 specs have mirrors and golden cases — no gaps. Mirror abstentions are modeled as the spec models them: String lookups are unpinned where the spec is silent (the winner returns `""` there in every run; recorded, never counted as disagreement), and `node_arity`'s unpinned kinds are held to the spec's `output >= 0` floor. A winner that fails to compile or run counts as total disagreement for that run. The comparison mechanics are pinned by 3 non-ignored tests (hand-built agreeing/disagreeing winners through the same compile+run path are detected on exactly the inputs where they differ). **Agreement matrix (3 runs; full per-spec × per-run table in `target/m2-synthesis/cross-validation.md`): golden cases 52/64, 51/64, 51/64; sweep pinned cases 308/539, 300/539, 389/539 — and the run-to-run variance is entirely the two test-less specs, because the 12 tested specs produced identical agreement numbers and identical disagreement sets in every run of two independent harness passes (6 runs total).** Per spec, stable across runs unless noted: `node_arity` 17/17 pinned + 8/8 golden; `is_literal_kind` 17/17 + 3/3; `is_infix_kind` 17/17 + 3/3 — **behavioral agreement is perfect exactly for the three Z3-verified winners**: tests ∧ proof ∧ execution now agree on precisely the same set. The five range/equation Bool specs agree only at and inside their tested points: `is_known_type_tag` 11/17, `is_known_node_kind` 11/18, `validate_kind_tag` 11/19, `max_depth_ok` 66/75, `line_width_ok` 102/110 — the per-test match's wildcard (`true`) is wrong on every spec-determined input on the untested side (e.g. `is_known_type_tag(-2) = true`, `max_depth_ok(1000) = true`); these are exactly the winners the candidate-encoding verifier REFUTES, now refuted by execution as well. The String lookups cover only their in-spec tested rows: `type_tag_name` 3/6 pinned (golden 3/6 — untested rows 2/3/7 return `""`), `node_kind_name` 4/8 (golden 4/8), `binop_symbol` 4/6 (golden 4/6), `print_node_kind` 2/8 (its winner covers exactly its 2 tested rows). Sharpest form of the gap: these winners measure tpr 1.000 — they pass every test the engine scores — while failing golden cases that exist in `tests/m2_proof.rs` but not in the `.morph` files, because tpr reads only the in-spec tests. The two test-less specs are semantically arbitrary, as predicted: `tags_compatible` sweep agreement 20/121, 31/121, 38/121 across the runs (90/121 in the first pass's draws), golden 3/6, 4/6, 3/6; `needs_parens` 23/100, 4/100, 86/100, golden 5/5, 2/5, 3/5 — one draw aced all 5 golden cases while agreeing on only 23/100 sweep cases. Classification: every pinned disagreement above is class (A) — the spec's constraints determine the answer (a single equation, or a guard row) and the winner is wrong there; class (B) is the 41 unpinned String-table cases per run, where the mirror abstains exactly where the spec is silent (no mirror-opines/spec-silent inputs exist in this codebase; the one asymmetry, `node_arity`'s floor, is enforced as pinned and agrees in all runs); class (C): no mirror defects found — one harness wart was found and fixed (the rustc failure excerpt initially surfaced a warning line as the failure reason). The first harness pass also surfaced two execution failures the second pass's draws did not reproduce, both root-caused by hand and neither a harness artifact: an `is_literal_kind` winner emitting `(- 0).abs()` — E0689 ambiguous numeric type, which fails the probe's `--crate-type lib` compile identically (a codegen emission defect; that draw's compile miss is the known 13/14 variance); and a `needs_parens` winner computing `(-1) / op` into a dead local — it compiles, then panics on every input with `op == 0`, including the golden cases (0,2) and (0,1): a winner whose body traps on legal inputs, invisible to tpr (the spec has no tests), to the return-type gate (division is Int-typed) and to Z3 (division by zero is totalized there — the verifier note's soundness caveat made concrete). **Gate consequence: "Same validation pipeline as M1" now runs end-to-end and item (d) is done as a measurement, but both M2 checkboxes stay open** — cross-validation shows the pipeline's winners do not yet implement their specs beyond the tested points for 11 of 14 specs, so "Both pass independently" cannot be honestly ticked. What stands between here and the gate is generalization, not more measurement: spec-exact candidates do reach the pool for the equation specs (the verifier proves them when they win their draws), but selection crowns the per-test match because tpr — measured only on in-spec tests — dominates the composite and a Z3 refutation costs only the 0.05 constraint weight. Plausible mechanisms, in order: (1) a refutation gate in selection, mirroring the completeness and return-type gates (a candidate the verifier REFUTES cannot win while an unrefuted one exists) — sufficient only where spec-exact candidates reach the final pool; (2) constraint-derived seeds: the guard tables and range expressions are fully present in the spec text, including rows no test exercises, so candidates built from the constraints (not only from the tests) would give (1) something to crown for the lookup/range families; (3) real variation operators (carried over, unchanged) for diversity beyond the seed shapes. Suite after this branch: 172 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored.

> **Refutation-gate follow-up (2026-11-20, branch `fix/refutation-gate`):** Phase 15's mechanism (1) is LANDED — and its measurement refutes the premise Phase 15 stated for it. Plumbing first: `verify_all` computed the full `VerificationStatus` per candidate and discarded it, keeping only the collapsed `verified` bool, so selection could not tell a refuted candidate from an inconclusive one. `CandidateImplementation::verification_status` now records the verdict at the exact point `verify_all` computes it (one source of truth — a verifier test pins the recorded status against `verify_one` on the decisive pair). The gate is the third in `select_best`, after completeness and return-type, mirroring their shape: a candidate whose recorded verdict is `VerificationFailed` cannot win while any survivor is unrefuted (Verified, Inconclusive, Malformed, or unverdicted); an all-refuted pool falls back to the old ranking unchanged; there is deliberately no Verified-preference beyond excluding the refuted. Tests pin all four semantics (refuted tpr-1.0 loses to an unrefuted lower-tpr candidate; all-refuted fallback; Inconclusive never gated out and no Verified-preference; a Verified tpr-1.0 candidate still wins, plus a pipeline integration test for `is_literal_kind`). **The gate does exactly what it specifies: zero of the 42 probe winners are refuted** (winner verdicts, newly recorded by the probe: 9 Verified — the same three specs as Phases 14/15 — and 33 Inconclusive; the Phase 14 baseline had 21 of 42 winners refuted). What it crowns instead is the problem: **new pool-level probe fields (`pool_unrefuted`, `winner_status`) show no Verified candidate reached the pool for 11 of 14 specs in ANY run** — the verified pool count is 0 for every range, lookup and test-less spec in all 21 spec-draws, and the only unrefuted company in those pools is Inconclusive: unencodable bodies (loops, unknown calls) that neither Z3 nor the interpreter can judge. Phase 15's "spec-exact candidates do reach the pool for the equation specs (the verifier proves them when they win their draws)" was winner-level inference; measured at the pool, synthesis never generates them for these families — the verifier proves them in unit tests when hand-built, but they are not in the pool to crown. Consequences, measured both instruments. Probe: winner tpr collapses honestly — 36/6/0 becomes 15× 1.000 (exactly the 3 verified specs + the 2 composition specs, whose all-Inconclusive pools make the gate a no-op, as designed), 7× 0.500 (the test-less neutrals + one), 12× 0.000, 8× partial; compiles 14/14, 14/14, 13/14 — the miss is the known E0689 `.abs()` codegen defect, seen again (run 3 `binop_symbol`, `1.abs()`; also one cross-validation rustc failure). Cross-validation (same harness; the 3 verified specs stay perfect — full sweep + golden in all runs — and the 2 composition specs are identical to Phase 15: `validate_kind_tag` 11/19 + 2/2 golden, `print_node_kind` 2/8 + 2/2 golden): the 7 families the gate actually touches get WORSE, because their new winners are inconclusive junk instead of refuted-but-tested matches. The String lookups now agree NOWHERE — golden 0/6, 0/8, 0/6 and sweep 0 in all runs (the winners return `""` for every input, failing even the tested rows the refuted winners covered). Range specs drop: `is_known_type_tag` sweep 9/17, 0/17, 9/17 (golden 3/5, 0/5, 2/5), `is_known_node_kind` 9/18, 9/18, 10/18 (golden 1/3, 1/3, 2/3), `max_depth_ok` 64/75, 64/75, 66/75 with golden 2/4 throughout, `line_width_ok` unstable (101/110 once; 10/110 and a runtime panic in the other draws). Totals: golden 52/51/51 → **29/23/29 of 64**; sweep pinned 308/300/389 → **332/238/246 of 539**, the run-1 sweep total propped up only by lucky test-less draws (`tags_compatible` 90/121, `needs_parens` 96/100 — the test-less pair remains pure draw variance: golden 2–3/6 and 0–3/5, sweep 0–96%). New execution classes appeared, all invisible to every existing signal: 2 non-terminating winners (`is_known_type_tag` run 2, `needs_parens` run 2 — the first harness attempt burned ~54 minutes at 100% CPU on a `while true` winner; the harness now caps each runner at 10s and records a timeout as total disagreement, its standing semantics, pinned by a mechanics test), and both Phase 15 execution defects recurred — E0689 `.abs()` (above) and trapping dead computations: discarded statements `(tag % tag);` and `((1 * width) / width);` that panic on legal inputs (tag/width = 0), 2 runner failures (exit 101: `type_tag_name` run 2, `line_width_ok` run 1). **Gate consequence: none — both M2 checkboxes were open and stay open; this phase changes what stands in the way.** Selection now carries all three gates (completeness, return-type, refutation), each proven to work as specified; the bottleneck is wholly pool composition. Mechanism (2), constraint-derived seeds, is no longer a follow-up that helps "where unrefuted candidates already reach the pool" — it is the missing precondition for 9 of 14 specs (4 range/equation: `is_known_type_tag`, `is_known_node_kind`, `max_depth_ok`, `line_width_ok`; 3 lookups: `type_tag_name`, `node_kind_name`, `binop_symbol`; 2 test-less: `tags_compatible`, `needs_parens` — zero verified pool candidates in every measured draw), and the only mechanism that can lift the 2 composition specs at all (their Inconclusive ceiling means the gate can never crown better than an unrefuted per-test match there; constraint-built seeds would at least be spec-shaped). The honest summary of the gate alone: for those 7 families it converts provably-wrong winners into unprovably-wrong ones — a real semantic improvement (no winner is a candidate Z3 can disprove) bought with worse measured agreement, and a trade worth completing rather than reverting: with constraint-derived seeds in the pool, the same gate crowns the spec-exact candidates it currently cannot find. Next blockers, in order: (1) constraint-derived seeds — the guard tables and range expressions are fully present in the spec text, including rows no test exercises; (2) the recurring execution defects — codegen `.abs()` on ambiguous numeric literals (E0689) and generated dead statements computing trapping divisions/remainders — now joined by a non-termination blind spot (loop bodies are unjudged by tpr, Z3 and the type verdict alike); (3) real variation operators (carried over, unchanged). Suite after this branch: 179 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored.

> **Constraint-seeds follow-up (2026-11-20, branch `feat/constraint-seeds`):** Phase 16's missing precondition is LANDED — candidates built from the spec's constraints now enter the pool, and the refutation gate finally has spec-exact bodies to crown. New seed family in `seed_population` (`seed_from_constraints`, provenance `ConstraintInduced { kind }`, reported by both the probe and the cross-validation harness), admitted only when the built body lies in the verifier's encodable subset — checked through a new verifier predicate, `body_is_encodable`, which is exactly the precondition `verify_one` needs before any postcondition can come back Proven, so an admitted seed is a candidate Z3 can fully judge, never one more Inconclusive pool member. Two builders, both reading only the postconditions (where the parser stores every `constraint:` line) and both conservative (unrecognized shapes yield no seed, never a guessed body). **Equation seed:** the first postcondition of the form `output == <expr>` (either orientation) whose right-hand side converts whole via the engine's existing `expr_to_ir` (a conversion that drops a subexpression into a Hole disqualifies it), does not mention `output` itself, and encodes — the candidate's body is that expression. **Guard-table seed:** for a spec with a single Int parameter, each postcondition of the form `<guard> || output == <literal>` is recognized *by evaluation*, not by matching one exact spelling: the Or's leaves must be exactly one output pin plus comparisons of the parameter against Int constants, and the row's pinned value is the unique integer at which every guard leaf is false (probed over the constants ±2 — outside the outermost constants every comparison's truth is stable and the extra probe points expose a false-region extending to infinity, so range-pinning rows are rejected); the first row wins a duplicated pin. The body is one match over the parameter, an arm per row, and a wildcard arm carrying the typed-literal default for the return type (the Phase 10 menu's first entry — the spec is silent off-table; the Phase 15 classification showed the mirrors abstain exactly there). Both families get the Phase 8 final-pool protection alongside the test-induced seeds (snapshot after seeding, re-admission before final scoring). **Coverage, per spec:** equation seeds for `is_known_type_tag`, `is_known_node_kind`, `is_literal_kind`, `max_depth_ok`, `line_width_ok`, `is_infix_kind` and the two test-less specs `tags_compatible` + `needs_parens` (their first truth signal of any kind); guard-table seeds for `node_arity` (its `output >= 0` constraint is skipped as a row, then proven of the seed like every other postcondition), `type_tag_name`, `node_kind_name`, `binop_symbol`; **no seed** for `validate_kind_tag` + `print_node_kind` — their equations call other specs, convert to IR but do not encode, and are skipped by the admission rule itself, not by special-casing. Five tests, red before the fix (API absent), pin the contract: the equation seed for `is_known_type_tag` is Verified by `verify_one` and wins `synthesize` at tpr 1.000 with the constraint's own body; the `type_tag_name` guard-table seed returns the pinned output on every row — evaluated through the IR interpreter, including the untested rows 2 → "Float", 3 → "Bool", 7 → "Option" — and is Verified; `node_arity`'s seed evaluates every pinned row plus the floor-satisfying default and is Verified whole; both test-less specs' equation seeds are Verified and reach the final pool; the composition specs and a floor-only spec get no seed. **Probe (3 runs × 14 specs): the winner tpr distribution is back to 36× 1.000 / 6× 0.500 (the test-less neutral) / 0× 0.000 — the Phase 15 numbers — but the winners are now the spec-exact bodies**: 36/42 winners are ConstraintInduced *and* Verified (12 per run: the ten tested seed specs at tpr 1.000 plus the test-less pair at their neutral 0.500), the composition winners are unchanged (Mutation, Inconclusive, tpr 1.000), refuted winners stay 0/42, and compiles are 14/14 in all 3 runs with no error classes. The Verified pool count — Phase 16's damning field — is ≥ 1 for 12/14 specs in *every* run (it was 0 for 11/14 specs in every Phase 16 draw); the composition pools remain structurally 0, because their postconditions call other specs and therefore do not encode for *any* candidate. **Cross-validation (3 runs, and a second harness pass identical in every cell): golden agreement 64/64 in every run** (Phase 15: 52/51/51; Phase 16: 29/23/29) **and sweep pinned agreement 525/539 in every run** (Phase 15: 308/300/389; Phase 16: 332/238/246). Per spec: twelve specs agree on every golden case *and* every pinned sweep case in every run — including the test-less pair at full agreement deterministically (`tags_compatible` 121/121 + 6/6 golden, `needs_parens` 100/100 + 5/5 golden; Phase 16 had them at pure draw variance, sweep 0–96%), the range specs at their full sweeps (`max_depth_ok` 75/75, `line_width_ok` 110/110 — Phase 16: 64–66/75 and 10–101/110 with a panic), and the String lookups on all rows including the untested ones, returning `""` on the unpinned cases in every run (the typed default; the mirrors abstain there). The only remaining disagreements in the entire matrix are the two composition specs, byte-identical to Phases 15/16 (`validate_kind_tag` 11/19 + 2/2 golden, `print_node_kind` 2/8 + 2/2 golden): their winners are still per-test matches under the Inconclusive ceiling, exactly as this phase's scope predicted. **Execution hazards: none recurred** — 42/42 winners execute in both harness passes (no timeouts, no trapping dead computations, no E0689), because the winning bodies are now terminating seed shapes; those defects remain live backlog for any pool where loop/random bodies can still win, which in practice now means the composition pools. **Gate consequence: the checkboxes stay open** (this phase does not tick them), but what stands in the way has narrowed to one item: the composition pair. Every earlier gate blocker has now landed — a scorer that executes the spec's tests (Phase 7), a verifier that encodes the candidate itself (Phase 14), gates that keep refuted/mistyped/holed bodies from winning (Phases 6/11/16), compiling codegen (Phases 4/5/13), spec-exact pool composition (this phase), and cross-validation itself (Phase 15, now at full agreement for 12/14 specs). Lifting the last two specs needs the verifier to encode spec-to-spec calls (inlining a callee's body or postconditions into the caller's check) — a verifier-semantics extension, not another selection or seeding mechanism — after which "Both pass independently" can be judged on a matrix with no spec-determined disagreements left. Remaining backlog, in order: (1) verifier encoding of spec-to-spec calls (the composition ceiling); (2) the execution-hazard blind spots (codegen `.abs()` E0689 on ambiguous literals, trapping dead computations, non-terminating loop bodies — unjudged by tpr, Z3 and the type verdict alike); (3) real variation operators (carried over, unchanged). Suite after this branch: 184 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored.
> **Verifier spec-calls follow-up (2026-11-20, branch `feat/verifier-spec-calls`):** the last M2 blocker from the constraint-seeds note is FIXED — spec-to-spec calls now resolve, on both sides of the verifier and in seed admission, through ONE shared resolver. `SpecIndex` (new, in the synthesis engine beside the Phase 17 builders) maps a module's specs by name and answers "what does this name denote" with exactly the body the callee's constraint seed would be built from: its first convertible equation body (`equation_rhs`, extracted from the seed builder so both read the same first equation), or its guard-table match when it has no equation — `node_kind_name` is the case that matters, and without the guard-table fallback `print_node_kind` would still denote nothing. The verifier carries the index (`Verifier::with_specs` / `with_index`; `Verifier::new()` keeps an empty index and judges exactly what it judged before) and threads an `EvalCtx` — index, visiting stack, depth — through both encoders. `sym_invoke` evaluates the callee body in a fresh environment binding the callee's parameters to the already-encoded argument VALUES — substituting values, not syntax, so capture is impossible by construction — and is shared by the body side (`sym_call`) and the constraint side (`encode_expr`), so a call denotes the same thing in a candidate and in the postcondition it is judged against. A name already on the visiting stack, or a call that would exceed `MAX_INLINE_DEPTH` (8, one constant shared by both resolution paths), is unencodable: Inconclusive, never a guessed value, never an unbounded expansion. Seed admission uses the same index twice over: `SpecIndex::inline_calls` resolves calls at IR level (argument substitution into the callee body, screened against pattern-binder capture, same visiting/depth discipline) so the admitted composition seed body is the RESOLVED callee body — the range check for `validate_kind_tag`, the lookup match for `print_node_kind` — which the interpreter can measure (tpr 1.000), selection can crown, and codegen can emit standalone (a raw call body would fail the cross-validation runner's rustc with E0425: the runner contains only the winner's own code); and admission itself is judged by the new `body_is_encodable_with` in the module context. The engine learns the module via `SynthesisEngine::with_module` / `synthesize_with_module`; standalone `synthesize` and registry-less verification are unchanged (the Phase 17 "composition specs get no seed" test runs registry-less and still passes). The probe and the cross-validation harness now run the module-aware pipeline; `morphic build` in `main.rs` still synthesizes a file's first spec standalone and is the one call site deliberately not rewired here. Nine tests, red before the fix (API absent): both composition seeds exist, are Verified, and win synthesis with their resolved bodies at tpr 1.000; a constant candidate for `validate_kind_tag` is REFUTED — the first refutation these specs can produce (registry-less it stays Inconclusive); a raw call body verifies (both sides resolve the same callee and meet); a body calling the WRONG callee (`is_literal_kind` for `validate_kind_tag`) is refuted exactly where the callees disagree (kind 5); the guard-table composition is judgable both ways (the per-test match is refuted on the untested Literal row, the full lookup verifies); a hand-built cyclic pair (a⇄b) is Inconclusive from both sides — body side and constraint side — with the test completing as the termination evidence; a 3-spec chain verifies transitively and a 12-spec chain exceeds the depth cap into Inconclusive. **Probe (3 runs × 14 specs): winner tpr distribution unchanged — 36× 1.000 / 6× 0.500 (the test-less neutral) / 0× 0.000; refuted winners 0/42; compiles 14/14 in every run — and the winners are now spec-exact for all fourteen specs: 42/42 winners ConstraintInduced AND Verified (was 36/42), the composition pair included. Cross-validation (two harness passes, identical): golden agreement 64/64 in every run; sweep pinned agreement 525/539 → 539/539 in every run — `validate_kind_tag` 11/19 → 19/19, `print_node_kind` 2/8 → 8/8, the other twelve unchanged at full agreement; the harness's disagreements section now contains only the spec-silent unpinned notes.** The cross-validation matrix therefore has NO spec-determined disagreements left: every pinned case of every spec agrees, in every run, under both instruments. Per this phase's discipline the M2 checkboxes are NOT ticked here — the gate decision is the next, separate step. Recorded caveats, kept distinct from the evidence: (1) inlining a guard-table callee imports its documented off-table default into the composed constraint — the pinned rows are exact (they are the callee's own postconditions), but off-table the composed check inherits the same typed-literal default the callee's own seed carries and the mirrors return (the Phase 15 classification); a hypothetical candidate returning something else off-table would be refuted on the strength of that default. No such candidate arises in these pools, and the executed cross-validation — which models the mirrors' abstention honestly — agrees 539/539 regardless; whether the gate accepts this stance is a judgment for the gate step, not a measurement. (2) The resolver takes a callee's FIRST convertible equation, mirroring the seed builder's preference order; a callee whose first equation were convertible-but-inexact while a later one is exact would inline the first — no M2 spec has two equations. (3) `main.rs` wiring (above) remains a one-line follow-up. Next blockers, in order: (1) the M2 gate decision itself; (2) real variation operators (carried over, unchanged: 5 of the 6 mutation helpers are literally empty functions, the sixth routes through whole-tree-clone `random_subtree`, and `mutate` is GA-only so it never runs at opt level 1); (3) codegen adjacent finds (carried over: `expr_to_rust`'s `"??"` fallback for unhandled spec-`Expr` shapes, `Filter` yielding `Vec<&T>`, `Reverse` emitting the `()`-valued `.reverse()`). Suite after this branch: 193 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored.

> **M2 GATE CLOSED (2026-11-20, branch `feat/cli-module-wiring`):** the last standalone call site is wired, and both remaining checkboxes are ticked on a final measurement taken on the post-wiring HEAD. The wiring: `morphic build` in `main.rs` still synthesized the file's first spec standalone (`synthesize` + registry-less `verify_all`), so through the shipped CLI a composition spec's winner was a test-memorizing match and its constraint calls resolved for nobody; it now collects all functions from the type-checked file and runs `synthesize_with_module` + `Verifier::with_specs` — the identical pipeline the probe and cross-validation harness run — with CLI behavior otherwise unchanged (still the first spec, same output shape, same opt-level handling), pinned by a new e2e test (`tests/m2_proof.rs::build_first_spec_resolves_module_calls`: a fixture whose FIRST spec calls a spec defined later in the same file; red before the wiring on exactly the memorizing-match signature, green after; the generated code is the callee's inlined range check, compiles under rustc, and its generated tests pass when executed). "Same validation pipeline as M1" is now true of every entry point, not only of the measuring instruments. **Final gate measurement (post-wiring HEAD): suite 194 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored; probe (3 runs × 14 specs): winner tpr 36× 1.000 / 6× 0.500 (the test-less neutral) / 0× 0.000, Verified winners 42/42, ConstraintInduced winners 42/42, refuted winners 0/42, all winners hole-free, compiles 14/14 in every run; cross-validation (two harness passes, byte-identical reports): golden agreement 64/64 and sweep pinned agreement 539/539 in every run, the disagreements section containing only the spec-silent unpinned notes — no spec-determined disagreement anywhere.** Every Phase 18 number holds after the wiring; nothing regressed. The gate's standing caveat is recorded as ACCEPTED with the gate: inlining a guard-table callee imports its documented off-table typed-literal default into composed constraints — the pinned rows are the callee's own postconditions and are exact, and the executed cross-validation, which models the mirrors' abstention honestly, agrees 539/539 regardless. **Gate: `validator.morph` and `printer.morph` both pass the full pipeline independently — M2 is closed.** What remains is not M2's: M3 (`codegen.morph`) is the next milestone, and the carried backlog stands as recorded (real variation operators; codegen adjacent finds — `expr_to_rust`'s `"??"` fallback for unhandled spec-`Expr` shapes, `Filter` yielding `Vec<&T>`, `Reverse` emitting the `()`-valued `.reverse()`; the execution-hazard blind spots for pools where loop/random bodies can still win). Suite after this branch: 194 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored.

### M3: Codegen (biggest risk)
- [x] Tulis `codegen.morph` (IR → Rust, 1 target dulu)
- [x] Ini komponen paling kompleks — perlu decomposition jadi sub-specs
- [x] **Gate**: Generated codegen produces byte-identical Rust output

> **Audit + decomposition map (2026-11-21, branch `feat/m3-codegen-specs`):** `src/codegen/mod.rs` is 2,295 lines: ~1,246 lines of emitter/support code and ~1,049 lines of unit tests. The Rust target's emitter surface, audited function by function (shape: (a) leaf mapping, (b) string assembly/formatting, (c) recursive IR tree walk, (d) top-level driver):
>
> | Function | Lines | ~Size | Shape | Role |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | `generate` | 12 | 9 | (d) | Public dispatch on target name |
> | `rust_ident` | 32 | 20 | (b) | Per-char identifier sanitization + keyword `_` suffix |
> | `is_rust_keyword` | 53 | 13 | (a) | 51-word keyword set membership (String → Bool) |
> | `generate_rust` | 66 | 92 | (d) | Whole-file assembly: doc header, signature, body, test module |
> | `ir_to_rust` | 310 | 196 | (c) | Statement-position IR walker |
> | `ir_to_rust_tail` | 507 | 33 | (c) | Tail-position dispatcher (classification + recursion) |
> | `ir_to_rust_expr_typed` | 549 | 10 | (b) | Expectation dispatcher; holds the String-literal ownership rule |
> | `expr_block_stmt` | 569 | 8 | (b) | Wraps statement rendering as a block expression |
> | `unsupported_expr` | 578 | 3 | (a) | Constant `panic!` template by shape name |
> | `expr_in_condition_position` | 587 | 15 | (a) | Wrap-in-parens classification over IRNode kind |
> | `ir_to_rust_expr` | 603 | 208 | (c) | Expression IR walker; contains the BinOp/UnaryOp/Collection leaf tables |
> | `ir_pattern_to_rust` | 813 | 25 | (c) | Pattern walker (small recursion, leaf literal subset) |
> | `ty_to_rust` | 1093 | 31 | (c) | Type walker; scalar leaf table + composite templates |
> | `constraint_to_rust` | 1162 | 14 | (c) | Constraint walker for precondition asserts (`"true"` catch-all) |
> | `expr_to_rust` | 1177 | 25 | (c) | Spec-`Expr` walker for generated test emission; own BinOp leaf table |
> | `debug_ir_string` | 1203 | 24 | (c) | Debug walker — serves the `"ir"` pseudo-target, not Rust output |
>
> Support, not emitters: `CodegenError` + impls (1228), `FunctionSpec::types()` (1084, stub returning an empty vec; its only caller is `generate_c`). Out of M3 scope (other targets only): `generate_c` (159), `generate_wasm` (197), `generate_python` (235), `generate_javascript` (268), `ir_to_c` / `ir_to_c_expr` (839/885), `ir_to_wasm` / `ir_to_wasm_expr` (910/929), `ir_to_python` / `ir_to_python_expr` (957/993), `ir_to_javascript` / `ir_to_js_expr` (1018/1055), `ty_to_c` (1125), `ty_to_wasm` (1140), `ty_to_python` (1150).
>
> **Slice plan** (dependency order — the walkers consult the tables, so tables come first; every slice lands specs + mirrors + golden tests before any synthesis run against it, and each ROAD anti-pattern holds: sub-functions ≤200 lines, tight specs with golden suites, no Z3 verdict trusted without cross-validation):
>
> 1. **Leaf mappings** — the (a) tables. **Slice 1 written this phase** (see below).
> 2. **Small template families** — `ty_to_rust` composite forms, `ir_pattern_to_rust`, `expr_to_rust` + `constraint_to_rust` (the spec-AST emitters behind generated asserts/tests), literal rendering (`IRLiteral` → text, incl. `f64` Display semantics), and the wrap classification of `expr_in_condition_position` (needs an IRNode-kind coding). **Specs + mirrors written (Phase 22, this branch) — see the slice-2 record below. Synthesis measurement is the next phase.**
> 3. **Name/text assembly** — `rust_ident`'s per-char rule, the String-ownership rule of `ir_to_rust_expr_typed`, and `generate_rust`'s assembly pieces (doc header, generics/params joins, test-module emission). **Specs + mirrors written (Phase 26, branch `feat/m3-slice3-specs`) — see the slice-3 record below. Synthesis measurement is the next phase.**
> 4. **Expression walker** — `ir_to_rust_expr`, sub-sliced by node family (operators; collections + iterator combinators; control forms; leaves/misc), each sub-slice ≤ ~200 lines of emitter. **Specs + mirrors written for the FULL walker (Phase 30, branch `feat/m3-slice4-specs`); synthesis measured in Phase 31 (branch `feat/m3-slice4-measurement`) — 27/35 seeded and spec-exact, the Int-keyed expression pins confirmed as the fifth-builder-family shape — see the slice-4 records below.**
> 5. **Statement walker** — `ir_to_rust` + `ir_to_rust_tail` + `expr_block_stmt`: the statement/tail position discipline, including the Collection statement-position collapse pinned in slice 1.
> 6. **Driver** — `generate_rust` whole-file assembly + `generate` dispatch; the byte-identical gate is judged here, over a corpus of candidates byte-compared against the original emitter. **Specs + mirrors written (Phase 40, branch `feat/m3-slice6-specs`) — see the slice-6 record below. Synthesis measurement and the gate itself are the next phase.**
>
> **Slice 1 (this branch):** `examples/self_host/codegen.morph` (module `Codegen`, 8 specs, 103 constraint rows) states each leaf table COMPLETELY — constraints carry the full mapping, in-spec tests stay representative (30 tests), mirrors + full-range golden tests live in `tests/m3_proof.rs` (13 tests: every row, off-range codes, keyword negative space, cross-table consistency). The specs: `rust_type_name` (guard table, 14 of 26 TypeRef codes pinned — the scalar rows of `ty_to_rust`; composite/named forms are slice 2), `rust_binop_symbol` (guard table, 17 of 19 IRBinOp codes; Append/Merge deliberately unpinned — they render as method calls), `rust_unaryop_symbol` (2 rows) + `rust_unaryop_method` (4 rows) + `rust_unaryop_form` (total 10-row family classification: prefix / method / list-part), `rust_collection_form` (total 5-row classification, expression position), `rust_keyword` (one equation over a String input: the exact 51-word set), `spec_binop_symbol` (total 14-row table incl. the `"?"` fallback rows). `morphic check` passes on the file; full suite 207 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored. No synthesis has been run against these specs yet — that is the next phase, on the M2 pattern (probe → verifier → cross-validation against these mirrors).
>
> **Slice 1 measurement (2026-11-21, Phase 21, this branch):** the M2 instruments were generalized to the Codegen module — `src/m3_synthesis_probe.rs` (module-aware pipeline × 3 runs × 8 specs; per-spec winner provenance/kind, tpr, verification status, seed presence + verdict, standalone rustc compile check) and `src/m3_cross_validation.rs` (winners executed against the `tests/m3_proof.rs` mirrors over the COMPLETE finite domains: Int codes −2..=30 for the seven code specs; for `rust_keyword` the 51-word positive set + 24 near-miss negatives — `gen`, `union`, `async␣`, leading space, uppercase variants, empty string, common non-keywords — under the M2 hang-proof 10s runner; spec-silent cases reported separately, never as disagreements). Mechanics tests pin the harness's honesty (disagreement detection incl. the total keyword spec, timeout, golden-inside-sweep) plus a standing diagnostic: every slice-1 seed body is recognized by the shared builders and encodable by the verifier in module context. Results were IDENTICAL in all 3 runs of both instruments (winners are seeds, so the outcome is structural, not draw luck):
>
> | Spec | Seed | Seed Z3 | Winner | tpr | Compiles | Golden | Pinned (per run) | Spec-silent behavior |
> |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
> | `rust_type_name` | guard_table | Verified | the seed | 1.000 | yes | 4/4 | 14/14 | 19 cases → `""` |
> | `rust_binop_symbol` | guard_table | Verified | the seed | 1.000 | yes | 5/5 | 17/17 | 16 cases → `""` |
> | `rust_unaryop_symbol` | guard_table | Verified | the seed | 1.000 | yes | 2/2 | 2/2 | 31 cases → `""` |
> | `rust_unaryop_method` | guard_table | Verified | the seed | 1.000 | yes | 3/3 | 4/4 | 29 cases → `""` |
> | `rust_unaryop_form` | guard_table | Verified | the seed | 1.000 | yes | 4/4 | 10/10 | 23 cases → `0` |
> | `rust_collection_form` | guard_table | Verified | the seed | 1.000 | yes | 3/3 | 5/5 | 28 cases → `0` |
> | `rust_keyword` | equation | Verified | the seed | 1.000 | yes | 5/5 | 75/75 (total) | — (no silent cases) |
> | `spec_binop_symbol` | guard_table | Verified | the seed | 1.000 | yes | 4/4 | 14/14 | 19 cases → `""` |
>
> Totals per run: winners 24/24 ConstraintInduced and Z3-Verified across the 3 runs (8/8 per run), tpr 1.000 for every winner, compiles 24/24, golden 30/30, pinned agreement **141/141 with zero pinned disagreements in any run**. Spec-silent behavior is the guard-table seed's typed-literal wildcard, deterministically: every String table returns `""` on all silent inputs and both Int classifications return `0`. Stated plainly: on `rust_type_name` composites (codes 14–25) the winner returns `""`, NOT the emitter's composite forms, and on Append/Merge it returns `""`, not method-call renderings — both spec-legal by slice-1 design (the spec deliberately pins nothing there) and exactly what slice 2 must pin if the winner is to match the emitter there.
>
> **Diagnoses — no slice-1 gaps found.** The existing M2 machinery covered every shape slice 1 contains, verified mechanism by mechanism: (1) the guard-table builder handled all seven Int-parameter tables, including the non-injective `rust_type_name` (codes 0 and 9 both pin `"i64"` — the builder dedupes pinned inputs, not outputs), the partial tables (2-row `rust_unaryop_symbol`, 4-row `rust_unaryop_method`), the total Int classifications, and `spec_binop_symbol`'s literal `"?"` fallback rows; (2) the equation path handled `rust_keyword` end to end — `expr_to_ir` converts the 51-way String-equality disjunction whole (no holes), the verifier's symbolic evaluator encodes String equality under Or and Z3 verifies the seed, and the interpreter measures it (tpr 1.000 is executed, not asserted): the module's first String-INPUT spec passes through every stage. (3) One observation, recorded not fixed (the emitter is frozen and this is not an engine gap): `generate_rust`'s emitted test module calls a String-input function with `&str` literals against its `String` parameter, so a winner file compiles standalone (lib/binary — the checks above) but its `#[cfg(test)]` module fails E0308 under `rustc --test`. String-input specs never existed in the M1/M2 sets; this is emitter test-emission behavior for slices 2–3 to reckon with when they spec the generated-test path. **Judgment: slice-1 synthesis is already closed by the M2 machinery — no engine or verifier work is a precondition for writing slice-2 specs.** Carried caveats: slice 2 introduces shapes slice 1 lacks (recursion into the type walker, name-carrying forms, string assembly), which the equation/guard shapes may not cover — the measurement-first pattern will show it on the same instruments; and the guard-table wildcard (`""`/`0`) is what stands in for every unpinned row, so slice-2 specs must pin the composites in constraints, as planned, or winners will keep returning the default there.
>
> **Slice 2 specs (2026-11-21, Phase 22, this branch):** 20 specs added to `examples/self_host/codegen.morph` (28 specs, 92 in-spec tests total), one hand mirror per spec plus assembly mirrors in `tests/m3_proof.rs` (27 tests), `morphic check` passes, full suite 225 passed / 0 failed / 6 ignored. **Representation rule:** the walkers render children first, so a composite form is a template over the children's already-rendered strings; the specs pin — completely, per family — WHICH template applies to each kind (total classifications), every fixed affix (prefix / separator / midfix / suffix tables), and every constant fallback. What they deliberately do NOT pin is the assembly act itself and the numeric text conversions, both inexpressible today (findings F1–F3 below). The specs: type templates — `rust_type_form` (total over all 26 TypeRef codes: scalar / name / wrap-one / wrap-two / tuple-join / array / function), `rust_type_prefix` (10 rows, incl. the unqualified `HashSet<` quirk), `rust_type_suffix` (10 rows, incl. the empty Function/Ref closers), `rust_type_separator` (5 rows), `rust_type_midfix` (the one `) -> ` row); literals — `rust_literal_form` (total over the 6 IRLiteral kinds), `rust_literal_bool` (the `b.to_string()` table both walkers share), `rust_literal_fixed_text` (Unit → `()`); patterns — `rust_pattern_form` (total, 5 kinds), `rust_pattern_fixed_text` (Wildcard → `_`), `rust_pattern_literal_form` (total, 6 kinds), `rust_pattern_literal_text` (the Float/Char/Unit → `_` rows), `rust_pattern_ctor_name` (the no-sub-patterns branch pinned exactly: output == name; multi-input, golden-tested in the proof file only); spec-expressions — `rust_expr_form` (total over all 13 Expr kinds), `rust_expr_fixed_text` (the seven `??` rows); constraints — `rust_constraint_form` (total over all 11 Constraint kinds), `rust_constraint_fixed_text` (the seven `true` rows), `rust_order_form` (total over the 4 OrderOp codes), `rust_order_separator` (` < ` / ` <= `); condition position — `rust_condition_wrap` (total over all 28 IRNode kinds, one equation: the ten block-like kinds wrap). The assembly mirrors compose the pinned pieces exactly as the originals do and golden-test the composed behavior (nested type composites two levels deep, empty and one-element tuples, raw unescaped strings, nested constructors and guards, nested binary expressions, empty `And` → `()`), so the composed behavior is cross-validated in Rust even where no spec can state it.
>
> **Language / machinery findings (for the language owner — no syntax invented, nothing faked):**
> **F1 — the spec language has no string concatenation (the slice's headline finding).** Every template assembly in the Rust emitter is `format!`-based concatenation of fixed text with rendered children; NONE of it can be stated as a spec constraint. `BinOp::Concat` exists in the spec AST (src/spec/ast.rs), the IR, the interpreter (src/synthesis/eval.rs evaluates it on strings) and the verifier (Z3 `String::concat`) — but the parser never produces it: `parse_expr_add` maps `+` to `BinOp::Add` and there is no other candidate operator. `+` on strings typechecks only because BinOp typing is coarse, and lowers via `binop_to_ir` to `IRBinOp::Add`, which the interpreter rejects on non-numeric operands and the verifier cannot encode (`sym_binop` has no (Add, Str, Str) case). Input class affected: every string-valued rendering with variable content — type composites, constructor/guard patterns, Call/BinOp spec-expressions, Not/Eq/Order/And constraints, the condition wrap, and String/Char literal quoting. A surface concat operator (or `+` overloaded on strings end-to-end) is a language-design decision for the user, in the same class as the functional_tokenizer items.
> **F1 RESOLVED (2026-11-21, Phase 25, branch `feat/parser-concat`)** — the user, as language owner, decided the same day ("Gas — buka concat di parser"). The surface syntax is **`++`**: `parse_expr_add` probes it before `+` and builds `BinOp::Concat`. It shares the additive level — left-associative, multiplicative-level operands, tighter than comparisons — because concat is the additive operation on its operand sorts and the additive loop is the one place a two-character operator arm fits without a new precedence function; `+` on numbers is unchanged byte-for-byte. The spelling was already the codebase's own: the LLM prompt renderer spelled `BinOp::Concat` as `++` all along. The footprint is one parser production (the parser is char-level — no token enum exists to extend) plus one typeck rule, so a rename stays cheap. Typing conforms to the sort-strict layers below, which defined Concat's meaning all along (interpreter: Strings and Lists; verifier: Z3 `String::concat` / list extension; selector's IR verdict likewise): the checker now types Concat precisely — String++String is String, List<A>++List<B> is List<A> unless the element types are provably different, and Int/mixed/mismatched operands are type errors at `morphic check` time — while every other operator keeps the existing coarse rule, and since Concat was previously unparseable no existing spec's check outcome changes. Decisive evidence: the wrap-one template `output == "Vec<" ++ inner ++ ">"` — unparseable before this phase ("Expected expression" at the second `+`) — now produces a Z3-Verified equation seed that wins selection, executes through the interpreter at tpr 1.000 (nested case included), and its winner's body is the constraint's own expression. One edge surfaced while driving it, recorded plainly: the frozen emitter renders IR Concat as Rust `+` and its string-ownership rewrite fires only on a bare literal in an expectation position, never on a Concat operand — so literal-first assemblies render `(("Vec<" + inner) + ">")`, which rustc rejects (E0369), while variable-first assemblies (`(inner + "!")`) compile; the concat e2e test splits the compile leg onto the variable-first fixture for exactly this reason. Extending the ownership treatment to Concat operands is emitter work for the walker/driver slices below, not a parser matter. Full suite 241 passed / 0 failed; M2 and M3 instruments re-run unchanged (M2: winners 42/42 Verified across the probe's runs, golden 64/64 + sweep 539/539 per run; M3: seeds 28/28 per run, golden 94/94 + pinned 301/301 per run).
> **F2 — arbitrary Int → decimal text is inexpressible.** The emitter renders Int literals and Array sizes with `to_string()`. Spec expressions have no Int → String conversion, and the domain is unbounded, so no finite table can pin it. Affected: `rust_literal_form` family 0 (classification pinned, rendering not), the Array form's size (separator `; ` pinned, size text not). The mirrors perform the conversion exactly (negative and i64-extreme goldens included).
> **F3 — `f64` Display text is inexpressible.** The emitter renders Float literals with `f.to_string()` (Display: shortest round-trip decimal, `1.0` → `1`). The spec language has no float formatting, so `rust_literal_form` family 1 is classification only; the mirror copies Display exactly (1.0 → `1`, 100.0 → `100`, 0.5, -2.25 goldens).
> **F4 — Char literals cannot be written in spec syntax.** A `Char`-typed input declares and checks, but a char literal in expression/test position is a parse error (probed 2026-11-21: `test { input: 'a' ... }` → "Parse error: Expected expression" at the literal). So no spec can name a Char value; Char literal rendering is classified + mirrored only (expression position: quoted template; pattern position: the `_` fallback). Same decision class as F1.
> **F4 RESOLVED (2026-11-21, Phase 45, branch `feat/parser-char-literals`)** — the user, as language owner, approved the same day ("Gas F4 — buka literal karakter di parser"), on the F1 pattern. What already existed below the parser (measured first, per the pattern): `TypeRef::Char` in the spec AST but NO Char expression variant; a dedicated `IRLiteral::Char(char)` in the IR; `Value::Char(char)` in the interpreter (which also yields Char from String indexing); and in the Z3 verifier Char is **Opaque** — `sym_literal` maps Char literals to `Sym::Opaque` like Float, Char parameters bind Opaque, and a String index at a constant in-range position yields Opaque — there is no honest Z3 sort for Char, so any operation needing a concrete sort is unencodable. The change conforms to exactly that: `Expr::CharLit(char)` joins the spec AST; `parse_expr_atom` gains the `'` arm (`parse_char_literal_value`: exactly one Unicode scalar; escapes `\'` `\\` `\n` `\r` `\t` and `\u{...}`, mirroring `escape_rust_char`'s world; empty / multi-char / unterminated / unknown-escape / non-scalar-`\u` are parse errors, test-pinned). Downstream arms only: typeck types it `Char`; `expr_to_ir` lowers to `IRLiteral::Char`; `expr_to_pattern` to a Char literal pattern; `value_of_expr` maps it to `Value::Char`; the verifier encodes it Opaque like Float; `expr_to_rust` renders it escaped via `escape_rust_char` (so a generated test module now calls `is_digit('0')`, not `??`); the prompt/engine formatters and the M3 gate's spec-expr glue render it as a char literal. Evidence: the pre-phase probe (`output == (c == '5')` + char tests) failed with "Expected expression" and passes `morphic check` now; the three tokenizer classifiers' constraint shapes parse verbatim in isolation (test-pinned); an `is_digit` classifier (range constraint + 15 char-literal tests) driven through the real build path crowns a hole-free winner at tpr 1.000, exact on every tested char. **Two caveats measured, recorded plainly:** (1) the pool holds TWO tpr-1.000 completions of the test-induced seed (wildcard `true` / `false` — no test exercises the wildcard arm) and, Char being Opaque to Z3, no constraint seed or proof can prefer the exact one; selection's tie-break crowns the wildcard-`true` completion, wrong on every untested char — while the spec-exact completion IS in the pool and executes exactly over a 774-char battery (U+0000–U+02FF + Arabic-Indic/fullwidth digits, emoji, U+10FFFF). Off-test exactness for Char classifiers therefore has no constraint-seed/Z3 backstop today; that is S4's standing caveat, in the guard-table-wildcard family. (2) At the rendered-Rust level, Char literal PATTERNS widen to `_` (the emitter's pinned pattern fallback for Float/Char/Unit — M3 slice 4), so a rendered test-induced Char match is not the exact classifier in Rust text; that is pre-existing emitter behavior, untouched here (the emitter is frozen; any change is a separate scoped decision). **S4 after F4:** the three classifiers' constraints + tests and `next_char`'s `'\n'` are unblocked; still gating S4 are the file's dialect layers (module wrapper, alias `;`, guard-style constraints — zero-decision re-authoring), method calls / Result inspection (`tokenize_one`/`tokenize_all` constraints), and record literals (in-spec test fidelity for the four record-input specs; mirrors remain the workaround). Suite: 343 passed / 0 failed, 8 ignored.
> **F5 — per-character string transformation is inexpressible (recorded 2026-11-21, Phase 26).** `rust_ident`'s sanitization step (src/codegen/mod.rs:33-48) maps a name char by char: keep `_` and alphanumeric chars, prefix `_` when the first char is an ASCII digit, replace every other char with `_`. The spec language has no character access at all — no indexing, no char predicates, no iteration over a string's chars, no string length — so neither the per-char keep/replace decision nor the leading-char rule can be stated, for any input class the rule touches. Affected input classes: every name containing a char outside the kept set (hyphens, spaces, dots — spec test names like "empty-list" are the standing example), every name starting with an ASCII digit, and names starting with a non-ASCII digit (kept, but with no prefix — the prefix test is ASCII-only). What specs CAN pin of `rust_ident`: the empty-name fallback (the step's output for `""` is exactly `"_"` — `rust_ident_empty`) and the keyword-suffix step over the sanitized string (`rust_ident_suffix`). The same gap covers `generate_rust`'s `", "` joins and doc splitting (iteration over list elements / lines): the templates over pre-joined / pre-split parts are pinned, the iteration itself is mirror-only. A char-access or iteration construct is a language-design decision for the owner, same class as F1/F4. (`++` does not rescue the Char literal template either: Concat's operands must both be String or both be List, so a Char value cannot enter a template — F4's classification stands.)
>
> **E0308 / String-ownership note (carried from Phase 21):** `expr_to_rust`'s StringLit rendering is pinned as-is (bare quoted raw text). The E0308 arises one level up: `generate_rust`'s test module (src/codegen/mod.rs:144-152) places `expr_to_rust(test.input)` directly in call position against the synthesized function's `String` parameters, and the spec-Expr side has no counterpart to the IR side's ownership rewrite (`ir_to_rust_expr_typed`). Recorded in `rust_expr_form`'s doc; any fix belongs to the driver slice or a language-owner decision — the emitter is frozen and this slice changes no emitter behavior.
>
> **Seed-shape note (measurement prediction, not a measurement):** 18 of the 20 new specs use shapes the Phase 17 builders already recognize (single-Int-param guard tables; `rust_condition_wrap`'s equation). Two do not: `rust_literal_bool` (guard rows over a Bool param — the guard-table builder reads Int params only) and `rust_pattern_ctor_name` (a guarded identity whose output pin is a variable, not a literal; also multi-param). The standing diagnostic `xval3_seed_shapes_recognized_and_encodable` was scoped this phase to the slice-1 set its own comment states (its slice-1 assertions are unchanged), because it iterates the whole module; generalizing the probe + cross-validation sweeps to slice 2 is the next phase's instrument work, and the two unrecognized shapes are its predicted first findings. No synthesis has been run against the slice-2 specs.
>
> **Surprises found while speccing slice 2** (behavior as-is; mirrors pin today's bytes): (10) `expr_to_rust` has NO FloatLit arm and NO UnaryOp arm — both render `??` (sharpens audit item 1: a float literal inside a spec test renders as `??` in the generated test's assertion); (11) `constraint_to_rust`'s `And` over zero parts renders `()` — the join template applied to an empty list, an assert that would not typecheck as a bool if it ever fired; (12) `ty_to_rust`'s one-element Tuple renders `(i64)` — no trailing comma, so not a valid Rust 1-tuple type — and its empty Tuple renders `()`, byte-identical to Unit's rendering.
>
> **Surprises found by the audit** (all behavior as-is; specs pin today's bytes, fixes are separate decisions): (1) there are TWO spec→Rust expression emitters with divergent operator tables — `ir_to_rust_expr` (IR, complete) vs `expr_to_rust` (spec `Expr`, only 8 BinOps mapped, the rest `"?"`, unhandled shapes `"??"`) — so the same operator renders correctly in a function body but as `?` inside the generated test assertions of the same file; (2) the IR BinOp table binds `".push"`/`".extend"` for Append/Merge into the operator variable, but those values are dead — the arms are special-cased first, so the table alone misleads; (3) collections are position-sensitive: Set/Map literals are fully-qualified `HashSet::from`/`HashMap::from` in expression position but collapse to `vec![..];` (element type silently dropped) in statement position; (4) qualification is inconsistent inside the Rust target — `ty_to_rust` renders `Set` as unqualified `HashSet<..>` while literals are fully qualified, and no import is ever generated, so a Set-typed signature would fail E0433; (5) `constraint_to_rust`'s catch-all renders `"true"` — Or constraints, GreaterThan orders and more are silently dropped from generated precondition asserts; (6) `ir_pattern_to_rust` renders only Int/Bool/String literal patterns — Float/Char/Unit patterns silently become the wildcard `"_"`, changing match semantics; (7) `FunctionSpec::types()` is a stub, so `generate_c`'s struct emission never fires (C-side, recorded for completeness); (8) float literals render through `f64` Display (`1` for 1.0) — deterministic, but the literal slice must copy Display semantics exactly or byte-identity fails on floats; (9) the 51-word keyword set matches no single Rust edition (has `try`/`async`, lacks `gen`), and `rust_ident` sanitization is applied by the Rust target only — C/Python/JS emit raw names.
>
> **Slice 2 measurement (2026-11-21, Phase 23, this branch):** the instruments were generalized to the whole module — `src/m3_synthesis_probe.rs` (module-aware pipeline × 3 runs × 28 specs; it already iterated the module, so its generalization is the recorded scope) and `src/m3_cross_validation.rs` (winners executed against the `tests/m3_proof.rs` mirrors over the complete finite domains: Int codes −2..=30 for the 25 Int-code specs; `rust_keyword` 51 positives + 24 near-miss negatives; `rust_literal_bool` the complete Bool domain; `rust_pattern_ctor_name` a 7-name × `has_subs` grid — the runner grew Bool-input and (String, Bool) pair-input shapes). Pinned vs spec-silent is defined by each spec's constraints, per spec per row (the harness's mechanics tests pin every spec's pinned total): **916 cases per run = 301 pinned + 615 spec-silent, 94 of them golden**. The standing seed-shape diagnostic now covers all 28 specs and asserts the two predicted-seedless shapes UNRECOGNIZED (below). No spec, engine, verifier, or emitter code changed this phase — harness and records only. Results were identical in structure in all 3 runs of both instruments (the 26 seeded specs are structural; the two seedless specs' winners are pool draws, characterized per draw below):
