> **Phase 63 (2026-11-21, branch `feat/m5-s3-heldout`) — M5-S3: the held-out new component, synthesized by the bootstrap binary; checkbox 1 is TICKED.** **The component:** a new module `RustVisibility` in a new file, `held_out/rust_visibility.morph` (3 specs, 9 in-spec tests), written fresh this phase in the module idiom — the Rust emitter's **item-visibility component**, the decision `generate_rust`'s signature head suppresses today: `rust_signature` states the fixed text `"pub fn "`, and no spec anywhere decides a qualifier. `rust_visibility_text` (guard-table: code 0 → `""`, 1 → `"pub "`, 2 → `"pub(crate) "`, the trailing space part of the rendering), `rust_visibility_is_known` (equation: `output == (code>=0&&code<=2)` — the assembly's rejection rule for unknown codes), and `rust_signature_prefix` (the composition stated as a table of final rendered text: `"fn "` / `"pub fn "` / `"pub(crate) fn "`, each row its code's qualifier followed by the `fn` keyword, the cross-spec agreement property stated in its doc). **Absence, verified not assumed:** greps over `examples/`, `tests/`, and the instruments find no visibility/qualifier/signature-prefix spec — the Codegen module (117 specs) hardcodes `"pub fn "` in `rust_signature` and has no visibility spec; the component is in no prior corpus (not the `examples/` inventory, not among M2's 14, M3's 117, M4's 143, or the tokenizer's 8) and was never a synthesis target in any probe, cross-validation, or gate before this phase. **Placement, structural:** the M4 gate's `enumerate_corpus` recursively scans `examples/` and asserts exactly 8 files / 143 specs, and the M3 instruments read fixed paths — so the file lives in a new top-level `held_out/` directory no corpus walker scans, and the held-out property is a fact of the tree, not a promise; the measurement's corpus guard re-counted the inventory (**8 files / 143 specs, unchanged**), which is also why the M3/M4 gates are unaffected by construction and were not re-run. The real CLI builds the file from its location (`morphic check` ✓ under both binaries; check is parse + typeck, configuration-independent).
>
> **Evidence (temporary instrument `src/phase63_measurement.rs`, removed after use on the Phase 50/57 precedent; report at `target/phase63/measurement-report.md`).** Extraction followed extract-once discipline — the real pipeline per spec (`synthesize_with_module` at `-O 1` → `Verifier::with_specs` → runner rescore → `select_best`), 3 draws per spec. **Crowns, identical in all 3 draws of each spec:** `rust_visibility_text` — `ConstraintInduced(guard_table)` (`de53fcf104307b31`), Z3 **Verified**, recorded tpr **1.000**; `rust_visibility_is_known` — `ConstraintInduced(equation)` (`4ac438af2d369a49`), **Verified**, tpr **1.000**; `rust_signature_prefix` — `ConstraintInduced(guard_table)` (`3d22c7f709bd0bbf`), **Verified**, tpr **1.000**. The G1 runner stage is NotTriggered for all three (every in-spec test is interpreter-bindable — the honest judging path, no compiled scoring needed). **The Phase 50 (D) criteria, point by point:** (1) *the flag-on binary builds the held-out spec end-to-end at `-O 1`* — the real `--features bootstrap-codegen` binary built the file in 3 draws, and, because the CLI builds a module's first spec (the whole module stays in scope for synthesis/verification), rotated copies seating each spec first were built the same way: all build. (2) *the produced implementation compiles as Rust* — every crown's flag-on rendering compiles whole under `rustc --test`, generated test module included, in every draw (instrument and real-binary outputs alike). (3) *the produced implementation passes the spec's in-spec tests, executed* — the generated tests report **3 passed / 0 failed for each spec in every draw** (file draws and per-spec rotations at the binary level; per-crown in the instrument). (4) *the same extracted winner rendered flag OFF vs flag ON is byte-identical* — per spec, per draw: original emitter == compiled adapter at 1,233 B (text), 702 B (is_known), 1,333 B (prefix); at the CLI level the flag-off binary's output file for the module's first spec is likewise byte-identical to the flag-on binary's (md5-equal across all 3 draws of each). **Mirror battery:** hand-written mirrors in the instrument, executed against the draw-0 crowns through compiled runners over the specs' pinned domains — `rust_visibility_text` 3/3 (codes 0..2; the spec pins nothing outside them, by design — `rust_visibility_is_known` is the stated rule there), `rust_visibility_is_known` 16/16 (codes −5..=10), `rust_signature_prefix` 3/3 — **22/22 full agreement**. **Draw stability:** the crowns are constraint seeds and deterministic — the same body in 3/3 draws per spec at the pipeline level, and byte-identical output files across the binary draws. One dialect fact recorded while authoring: a negative Int literal is not parseable in test-input position (`input: -1` → "Expected expression"), so `rust_visibility_is_known`'s below-range behavior is carried by its equation and the executed battery (−5..=−1 included), not by an in-spec test.
>
> **One finding, measured before it was routed around — Concat over an inlined table renders unowned (E0369).** `rust_signature_prefix` was first stated as an equation over a call — `output == rust_visibility_text(code) ++ "fn "` — and that stating crowned its equation seed (`91c3bd3fd5f31d8a`, Verified, tpr 1.000, rendered byte-identically by both emitters), whose body inlines the callee's guard-table as a match of `&str` arms in Concat's left-operand position: `(match code { 0 => "", 1 => "pub ", 2 => "pub(crate) ", _ => "" } + "fn ")` — `&str + &str`, rustc **E0369**, at the instrument level AND in the real flag-on binary's output (rotated build). Both emitters produce it identically, so it is the shared rendering of that body shape, kin to the Phase 25/29 E0369 Concat-ownership family but distinct from it: the operand is a compound expression (an inlined Match), not a literal or a Var, so no ownership treatment fires. Per this phase's rule — evidence + the new spec file + docs, no production fixes inline — **no emitter code was changed**; the spec was restated in the module's own leaf-table idiom (tables state final rendered text, cf. `spec_binop_symbol`), its doc states the cross-spec agreement property the equation would have enforced structurally, and the mirror battery discharges that property executed (prefix rows == visibility rows ++ `"fn "` on every pinned code). The finding joins the emitter backlog as recorded: a Concat whose left operand is an inlined table-valued call renders unowned in both emitters. **Blast radius:** M2 probe + cross-validation pass; M3 probe pass (per run: seeds 114/117, seed-crowned 114, Verified winners 117/117, compiles 117/117) and M3 cross-validation pass (3 runs, every row full agreement, pinned 2,997/2,997 per run, zero disagreements); tokenizer probe + cross-validation pass (classifiers 774/774); corpus counts asserted unchanged (8 files / 143 specs). **Zero production code changes** — the final diff is the new spec file plus these docs (`src/main.rs` byte-identical after the temporary instrument's removal). Suite **410 passed / 0 failed / 25 ignored in BOTH configurations** — identical to Phase 62. **Checkbox 1 is TICKED above:** all four Phase 50 (D) criteria hold for a component the compiler did not have, synthesized end-to-end by the bootstrapped binary through its synthesized codegen stage. **What remains in M5:** checkbox 4 only — S4 (the SC3 recorded measurement + no-regression + the gate closing). > **Phase 64 (2026-11-21, branch `feat/m5-s4-gate`) — M5-S4: the SC3 recorded measurement + the no-regression pass + the M5 GATE CLOSING. Checkbox 4 is TICKED; the M5 gate is CLOSED — full bootstrap, no regression.** **(1) SC3, measured in the Phase 50 design's form, with one recorded deviation** (new ignored instrument `m5_s4_sc3_benchmark`, `src/codegen/m5_s4.rs`; report at `target/m5-s4/sc3-report.md`). The timed operation is the codegen render — the only stage that differs between the configurations; synthesis is identical original code on both sides, and extraction / corpus construction are never timed. The original side is the per-kind walk dispatch the M3 corpus builders themselves use to produce `case.original`, verified against it on every case in the warmup; the synthesized side is the compiled adapter calling the checked-in artifact's winner functions directly (`render_rust_synthesized` for whole files) — the shipped flag-on path, not the interpreter composition (a gate measurement device, never a shipped form). The warmup asserts both sides byte-identical on every case (M3: 2,573 cases / 356,070 B rendered; M4: 143 files / 146,598 B), so the benchmark provably times two renders of the same bytes. Seven interleaved rounds per side, alternating start side, medians reported: **M3 corpus — original 20.6 ms, adapter 33.4 ms, ratio 1.620×** (rounds: original [20.7, 20.6, 20.4, 23.5, 20.2, 20.6, 20.2] ms, adapter [33.9, 33.1, 34.3, 33.4, 33.6, 33.1, 32.8] ms); **M4 corpus — original 4.2 ms, adapter 6.1 ms, ratio 1.445×**; **combined 24.8 ms vs 39.5 ms — ratio 1.590×, within the SC3 ≤ 2× threshold**. Per owner decision D3 this is a recorded measurement with its baseline, not a hard gate item — the number is published either way — and as measured the compiled path meets the threshold the Phase 50 audit called plausible: the adapter's render is the same string-building walk as the original's, its premium the winner-call and kind-code overhead. **The deviation, recorded plainly:** the design specifies the release profile (the shipped profile; every number quoted before it is debug and is not the baseline). In this sandbox a release build cannot exist: z3-sys's build script (the `gh-release` feature) must download the Z3 4.16.0 release and fails — `Could not find release for z3-4.16.0`, no network path to the release — while the debug build works only through the planted build cache in `target/debug/build`; transplanting that cache into the release profile would be working around the cache rule, so it was not done. The measurement therefore runs in the **debug profile** — the profile every number in this project has been measured in — with both sides in the same profile and the same session, so the ratio (the SC3 quantity) is measured like-for-like; the absolute times are debug numbers and are reported as such. A release-profile re-measurement is the recorded follow-up if a release build ever becomes possible in this sandbox. **(2) No-regression — the gate's second half, all at current counts.** Full suite **410 passed / 0 failed / 26 ignored in BOTH configurations** (the Phase 62/63 baseline is 410/25: passed identical; ignored +1 is this phase's benchmark instrument). The standing gates re-run one final time: **M3 gate PASS** (2,573/2,573 byte-identical, mode B; mode A 2,233 with all 340 divergences the recorded F5 class, A-unreconciled 0; 0 execution / transcription failures); **M4 gate PASS** (attempted 143, identical 143, divergent 0, errors 0 of every kind; pipeline runs exactly 143); **S1 byte-identity PASS** (fresh extraction + render produced 111,558 B == checked-in 111,558 B); **S1 equivalence PASS** (M3 corpus 2,573/2,573 three-way — adapter == original == interpreter composition; M4 corpus 143/143 three-way, 143 pipeline runs); **S2 roundtrip (a) PASS** (4/4 byte-identical in EACH configuration). M2 probe + cross-validation pass at standing tallies (zero pinned disagreements; golden 64/64 + pinned 539/539 character); tokenizer probe + cross-validation pass (the three Char classifiers 774/774 in every run). **Behavioral spot check** (the Phase 62 closing table stands as the full behavioral evidence; this confirms nothing drifted since): one real-binary build of `sort.morph` at `-O 1` per configuration — both binaries crown the scan-form template, both outputs compile whole including the generated test module, and the generated tests pass **6/6 in each configuration**. One observation from the spot check, recorded because it was demonstrated live on its first draw: the G1 runner stage shells out to `rustc`, so on a PATH without rustc the stage degrades to the fallback scoring exactly as Phase 58 designed, and a junk crown (an E0689-class body) is then possible; with rustc on PATH — as in every instrument run in this project — the stage fires and the template crowns. The degradation is the recorded design, not a defect found here; it is written down so the binary's rustc-on-PATH expectation is on the record. **(3) The gate evidence, assembled.** Checkbox 1 — the held-out `RustVisibility` component, synthesized end-to-end by the flag-on binary through its synthesized codegen stage, all four Phase 50 (D) criteria (Phase 63). Checkbox 2 — the bootstrap binary under the audit's definition and owner decision D2: the original compiler with the checked-in synthesized Codegen artifact seated in the Rust emitter's place through the compiled adapter, switched by the `bootstrap-codegen` Cargo feature, the original kept and reachable with the flag off (Phases 51–52). Checkbox 3 — the roundtrip in the audit's measurable form: extract-once byte identity at the dispatch (S2 (a), re-proven above) plus the behavioral leg, every drawn sort output in both configurations compiling whole and passing 6/6 (Phase 62; spot-checked above). Checkbox 4 — this phase: SC3 recorded at a combined 1.590× (within the ≤ 2× threshold) and the no-regression pass above. **M5 CLOSING SUMMARY.** *What the bootstrap binary is:* the original Morphic compiler — parser, typechecker, synthesis engine, Z3 verifier, and selector all original, as no milestone ever specced them as self-host targets — with exactly one stage replaced: its Rust codegen, behind the `bootstrap-codegen` feature flag, rendered by a compiled module the compiler itself synthesized from `examples/self_host/codegen.morph` (117 specs; the checked-in artifact `src/codegen/synthesized_codegen.rs`, 111,558 B), seated through the hand-written adapter whose nine shim items are the mirror completion the M3/M4 gates judged (owner decision D1 — the mirror-completed composition, shipped). *What was proven:* the synthesized stage is byte-identical to the original emitter over every corpus the project can build (2,573 M3 cases and 143 whole files, three-way against the interpreter composition it transcribes); the flag-on binary synthesizes a component it was not built from and renders it byte-identically to the original; its sort roundtrip is behaviorally correct on every measured draw in both configurations; its codegen render costs a recorded 1.590× the original's; and the full suite plus every standing gate pass unchanged in character. *The recorded residue — the honest remainder, none of it discharged by this gate closing:* **G4**, fn-typed values in the IR interpreter (sort's and binary_search's in-spec tests still cannot bind there; the G1 compiled-runner stage is the standing judge instead); **G5**, binary_search's generation discipline (its template is crowned at recorded tpr 0.000 and does not compile — computed indices are i64 and cannot index a Vec, the spec language having no usize, and a by-value comparator applied to an Index place fails E0277; both blockers named in Phase 59, and the Phase 62 Clone bound is neutral for them, as predicted); **G7**, the five mutation stubs and the GA/LLM decoupling (opt ≥ 2 still aborts in the LLM seed in the default build, Phase 57); **is_sorted's judging-coverage wall** (its correct AdjacentScan template is in every pool at compiled 5/5 but cannot crown through interpreter judging — admitting Template provenance to the runner trigger is a one-line change held for an owner decision since Phase 60); **the Phase 63 emitter finding** (a Concat whose left operand is an inlined table-valued call renders `&str + &str` — E0369 — in both emitters identically; the compound-operand kin of the Phase 25/29 Concat-ownership family, joining the emitter backlog unfixed); the **tokenizer's standing state** (three Char classifiers exact by executed agreement; the M1 formal gate still open on its recorded structural gaps — no field-access node in the IR, no method calls / Result inspection in spec expressions); and the **SC3 profile caveat** in (1). **Suite: 410 passed / 0 failed / 26 ignored in BOTH configurations.** Local branch only, not pushed.

## 🚀 V0.6 ROAD — WASM Playground (opened 2026-11-21)

### W0: Scope audit

- [x] Audit what “Morphic in the browser” can honestly mean (Phase 65)
- [x] Owner scope decision (D1 below) — **D1–D6 all ratified as recommended, 2026-11-21**
- [x] W1: Core extraction + WASM build — **Phase 66, gate passed (below)**
- [x] W2: Check playground MVP — **Phase 67, gate passed (below)**
- [x] W3: Interpreter-judged synthesis — **Phase 68, gate passed (below)**
- [x] W4: Worker, performance, and UX hardening — **Phase 69, gate passed (below)**
- [x] W5: Public static release — **Phase 70, gate passed (below)**
- [x] **Gate**: A static browser build runs the owner-approved scope from the same compiler codebase, with every unavailable native capability labelled rather than simulated

> **Phase 65 (2026-11-21, branch `docs/v06-playground-audit`) — v0.6 WASM PLAYGROUND AUDIT: scoping only; no production code changed.** The distinction that governs everything below: Morphic already has a WASM **output** target — `codegen::generate` dispatches `"wasm"` to `generate_wasm`, which renders WebAssembly **text** (WAT) from a candidate (`src/codegen/mod.rs:12–22`, `376–412`). The v0.6 item is the inverse direction: the **compiler itself compiled to WASM and running in a browser**. Those are different artifacts, different blockers, and different promises; the playground must never use the existence of the first as evidence for the second.
>
> **(A) Crate shape — today there is nothing for a browser entry point to link against.** `Cargo.toml:9–11` declares exactly one target, `[[bin]] name = "morphic"`, and there is no `[lib]` or `src/lib.rs`; a spike `cargo build --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` answers, verbatim, `error: no library targets found in package 'morphic'`. All modules are private children of the binary root: `src/main.rs:6–21` declares the five core modules (`spec`, `llm`, `synthesis`, `verify`, `codegen`) plus twelve test/measurement modules. The CLI's Build path is also the only assembled pipeline (`src/main.rs`, Build arm): `std::fs::read_to_string(input)` → parse → typecheck → `synthesize_with_module` → `Verifier::with_specs(...).verify_all(...)` → `runner::rescore_finalists(...)` → `select_best(...)` → `codegen::generate(...)` → `std::fs::write(output)`. A `morphic_wasm` entry therefore needs a new library API whose inputs and outputs are strings/DTOs, not paths; extracting that API is W1, not a packaging detail.
>
> **Module dependency graph (production paths; test-only references omitted).** `spec::{ast,parser,typeck}` is self-contained inside the crate (AST types use `serde`). `synthesis::eval` depends on `spec::ast` and the IR types owned by `synthesis::engine`. `synthesis::engine` depends on `spec::ast`, `synthesis::{eval,templates,selector}`, and — critically — `verify::verifier::{VerificationStatus, body_is_encodable_with}`. `synthesis::selector` depends on `spec`, engine types, and `VerificationStatus`. `synthesis::runner` depends on `codegen`, `spec`, engine, and selector. `verify::{smt,verifier}` depends on `spec`, engine, and the `z3` crate. `codegen` depends on `spec` and engine; `codegen::bootstrap_adapter` additionally depends on codegen's own helpers and the checked-in synthesized artifact. `llm::{client,prompt,parser}` depends on `spec` and `reqwest`; the engine does **not** import the `llm` module — its `call_llm` is feature-gated inline code (`src/synthesis/engine.rs:1325–1366`) — but `main.rs` still compiles the whole `llm` module into the binary. `synthesis::interactive` is CLI furniture (`colored`, `indicatif`, sleeps), not pipeline.
>
> **(B) WASM buildability inventory — measured in a throwaway worktree, not inferred.** Method: detached worktree at `c22fca4`, separate `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` outside both the worktree and the repository; the main checkout's `target/` was never cleaned, reused as the spike cache, or modified. The sandbox initially lacked the target (`rustup target list --installed` showed only the host); after `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`, four builds were run. **Build 1, shipped defaults:** `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` fails before Morphic code is reached — `getrandom v0.2.17` emits `compile_error!("the wasm*-unknown-unknown targets are not supported by default, you may need to enable the \"js\" feature…")`. `cargo tree -i getrandom@0.2.17` shows two parents: direct `rand v0.8.6 → rand_core v0.6.4` (the engine's RNG) and `ring → rustls → reqwest` (the LLM client's TLS path). **Build 2, defaults + a spike-only `getrandom/js` feature unification** (scratch-manifest change, not proposed as the design): the build now reaches the real native wall — `z3-sys v0.11.0`'s build script panics at its `build.rs:123`: **`Unsupported architecture: wasm32`**. The same script only maps `x86_64`/`aarch64` × Windows/Linux/macOS, downloads a Z3 GitHub-release archive with blocking `reqwest`, unzips it, and emits a static native `z3` link (`build.rs:92–165`). **Build 3, `--no-default-features` + the same spike-only getrandom fix:** dependencies compile, then the Morphic binary fails with **70 errors** in exactly two classes — `reqwest::blocking` cannot be found at `src/llm/client.rs:81,86` (reqwest itself notes the item is `#[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]`), and `z3` is unresolved throughout `src/verify/smt.rs` and `src/verify/verifier.rs`. That second class is an architecture finding, not a target quirk: `Cargo.toml:13–18` makes `z3` optional behind default feature `z3-support`, but **no source file cfg-gates the verifier** — the feature can remove the dependency, it cannot produce a no-Z3 verifier. **Build 4, stage isolation:** a spike-only `src/lib.rs` containing only `pub mod spec;` compiles for `wasm32-unknown-unknown --no-default-features` (debug `libmorphic.rlib`, 3.8 MB) — the parser/typechecker/AST stage is portable at source level today; this proves that stage's code, not the package graph around it.
>
> **Blocker inventory — HARD means “cannot exist in the standard browser build”, not “nobody could ever port the underlying technology”.**
>
> | # | Blocker | Evidence | Class |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | H1 | **Z3 via `z3-sys`** | Build 2 panic (`Unsupported architecture: wasm32`); gh-release script downloads + statically links a native archive; `src/verify/smt.rs:31–45` is a direct `z3::Solver` session | **HARD** for `wasm32-unknown-unknown` v0.6 — feature out the solver; a separate Z3-to-WASM port is a different project |
> | H2 | **Compiled-runner scoring (G1)** | `src/synthesis/runner.rs:130` imports `std::process::{Command, Stdio}`; the stage creates temp dirs/files (`246–267`, `438–446`), spawns `rustc` and the compiled candidate, and polls with `std::thread::sleep` (`760–790`) | **HARD** — browsers expose no process spawn, no `rustc`, and no general temp filesystem |
> | H3 | **LLM seed path as shipped** | Engine seed order calls LLM whenever `use_llm` (`src/synthesis/engine.rs:571–573`); opt levels 2–4 set `use_llm: true` (`413–443`); with neither backend feature, `call_llm` returns `No LLM backend enabled`; `llm-local` is native `llama-cpp-2`, `llm-remote` builds a Tokio runtime and `block_on`s a localhost Ollama POST (`1335–1361`) | **HARD** for a client-only build — exclude it; browser access to a user's localhost model, API keys, and CORS are product problems, not glue |
> | H4 | **CLI filesystem contract** | Build/Check take `PathBuf`s and read files; Build writes an output file; Init creates directories/files (`src/main.rs`, command arms) | **HARD as an interface, SOFT as code** — replace with source-string-in / result-string-out; downloads/uploads belong to JS |
> | S1 | **`rand`/`getrandom` backend** | Build 1 compile error; direct parent is `rand 0.8` used pervasively as `rand::thread_rng()` in the engine | **SOFT** — target-specific `getrandom` `js` backend, or (recommended for reproducibility) an explicitly seeded RNG injected by the API |
> | S2 | **`reqwest::blocking` in `llm/client.rs`** | Build 3, E0433 ×2; reqwest gates `blocking` out on wasm32 | **SOFT** — the wasm library graph must not contain the `llm` module at all |
> | S3 | **Verifier source coupling** | Build 3's unresolved-`z3` class; engine also calls the verifier's pure `body_is_encodable_with` while constructing constraint seeds (eight call sites in `engine.rs`) and selector needs only the `VerificationStatus` type | **SOFT architecturally** — split verdict types + encodability (pure) from the Z3 session (native); add a no-Z3 backend whose verdict is honestly `Inconclusive`/unavailable |
> | S4 | **CLI/interactive furniture** | `clap`, `colored` in `main.rs`; `synthesis::interactive` uses `colored` + `indicatif` + `std::thread::sleep` | **SOFT** — stays in the native binary target; never enters the library graph |
> | S5 | **Test/gate/measurement modules** | Binary root declares twelve; codegen's gate modules are `#[cfg(test)]` and use fs/process/rustc | **SOFT** — a lib target simply does not declare them; native tests keep them |
> | S6 | **Wall-clock budgets** | `Instant::now()` drives the engine's elapsed accounting and the verifier timeout path; no compile error surfaced, and no browser run exists yet to prove the runtime behaviour | **SOFT / UNPROVEN** — W1 must smoke-test timing in a real browser worker; until then playground budgets are iteration/population caps, not milliseconds |
> | — | **`rayon`** | Declared in `Cargo.toml`, but no production `rayon`/parallel-iterator use was found; the spike build even warns it is an unused dependency | **NOT A BLOCKER today** — v0.6 should stay single-threaded in a Web Worker and not import an atomics/shared-memory requirement it does not need |
>
> **(C) Portable-stage map — each row follows from (A)+(B), and “portable-minus” names the exact seam owed.**
>
> | Pipeline stage | Verdict | Ground |
> |---|---|---|
> | Parse | **Portable** | Build 4: the whole `spec` module compiles to a WASM rlib |
> | Typecheck / `morphic check` | **Portable-minus CLI** | Same module proof; only the path-reading wrapper is native |
> | Interpreter evaluation (`synthesis::eval`) | **Portable-minus engine types** | `eval.rs` itself uses only AST + IR types + `HashMap` and is total/`NotEvaluable`-honest; it evaluates a **candidate body**, so it is portable wherever a body exists |
> | “Run the spec's tests” with no candidate | **Not a stage that exists** | `run_spec_tests(body, spec)` requires an IR body. A check-only UI can validate tests and report bindability, but must not claim it executed them against an implementation unless it first constructs one — and constructing the test-induced/constraint body is already mini-synthesis |
> | Constraint seeds | **Portable-minus verifier split (S3)** | The seed builders are AST/string computation, but they consult `body_is_encodable_with`, currently housed with the Z3 verifier |
> | MCTS / GA synthesis with interpreter judging | **Portable-minus S1+S3, LLM-free config only** | Search, templates, and `evaluate_candidate` are in-process computation; the blockers are `thread_rng` (S1), the encodability/status coupling (S3), and the LLM seed (H3). The CLI's default `-O 3` is therefore **not** the browser configuration |
> | Z3 verification | **Native-only** | H1 |
> | Compiled-runner final scoring | **Native-only** | H2. Consequence, stated plainly: candidates whose bodies the interpreter cannot evaluate (loop/scan bodies — the Phase 59–62 sort / is_sorted / count_matching territory) lose their native ground-truth judge in the browser; the UI must show evaluated vs not-evaluable counts and must not silently reuse the native crowns' claims |
> | Selection | **Portable-minus S3** | Pure ranking except for its `VerificationStatus` type dependency |
> | Codegen — Rust / C / WAT / Python / JS / IR | **Portable** | Every route of `generate` is a string walk over `(candidate, target)`; the bootstrap adapter + synthesized artifact are likewise compiled Rust string code. The **WAT route emits text only** — assembling or running that WAT in-browser is extra machinery, not a current capability |
>
> **(D) Playground shape options, priced.** Effort is relative scope (S/M/L/XL), because the project has no velocity baseline that would make calendar numbers honest; “ops” is the continuing cost after ship.
>
> | Option | Scope | Build cost | Ops | What the user gets / what it costs in truth |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | **(a) Full client-side** — editor + check + LLM-free synthesis judged by the interpreter + executed-test evidence + codegen preview | **L** | W1–W5 below | **Zero server**; static hosting only | The real playground and the only zero-ops full demo. Truth cost: no Z3 verdicts and no compiled-runner judging — verdict UI must say “formal verification unavailable in this build” and show test coverage denominators exactly |
> | **(b) Check-only** — editor + parse/typecheck (+ test inventory/bindability) | **M** | A subset of W1–W2 | Zero server | Fastest honest browser artifact and the right first ship **inside** (a); as the final scope it demos the language's front door, not its reason to exist (synthesis), and its “run tests” wording is constrained by the not-a-stage finding above |
> | **(c) Browser front-end + native backend service** | **XL** | (a)'s front-end **plus** API service, job queue, and a sandbox that can safely run `rustc` + generated binaries on strangers' input | **Perpetual**: hosting bill, patching, rate limits, abuse response, sandbox escapes are a security programme | Full native parity (Z3 + runner) is the only gain. The project has no server today — no hosting, no service code, no Pages/API workflow (`.github/workflows/` holds only CI + native release) — so this is a new product surface, not a deployment detail |
> | **(d) Staged local-first (delivery strategy for (a))** | **L total, first usable ship at M** | (b) ships as W2, synthesis lands as W3 | Zero server | Same end state as (a) with an honest intermediate artifact; the staging is the recommendation, not a fourth scope |
>
> **Recommendation: (a), delivered as (d).** It is the only option that is both a real playground and free to operate; its two truth costs (no Z3, no compiled runner) are label-and-denominator problems the UI can discharge honestly, whereas (c)'s cost is a permanent service and (b)'s saving is bought by not demonstrating synthesis.
>
> **(E) Design for the recommended shape — one codebase, two shells.** (i) **Library extraction:** add `src/lib.rs` owning the core modules; `src/main.rs` shrinks to the CLI shell over a string/DTO API (`check_source(&str)`, `synthesize_source(&str, config)`, `render(candidate, target)`); native CLI behaviour and outputs stay byte-identical, proven by the standing suite. (ii) **Feature seams, following the `bootstrap-codegen` precedent** (one codebase, cfg at the dispatch): keep `z3-support` as the native default and give it a real no-Z3 counterpart — verdict types + `body_is_encodable*` move to a pure `verify-core` layer, the Z3 session sits behind the feature, and the no-Z3 `verify_all` marks every candidate `Inconclusive` with reason `formal verification unavailable in this build` **without overwriting the interpreter-measured scores**; gate `synthesis::runner` behind a new default-on-native `compiled-runner` feature with a no-op-plus-`Unavailable` outcome in the WASM build; exclude `llm` and `interactive` from the library's WASM graph entirely and force `use_llm: false` in the playground config; make the CLI's fs/process surface binary-only. (iii) **RNG:** prefer an API-supplied seed driving a deterministic RNG for playground runs (reproducible bug reports, pinnable gate cases); the target-specific `getrandom/js` unification from Build 2 is the fallback, recorded as spike-proven but not chosen silently. (iv) **Glue:** `wasm-pack` + `wasm-bindgen` (with `serde`-shaped DTOs) over a hand-rolled ABI — the API is string- and structure-heavy, and hand-rolling pointer/length plumbing is false economy with unsafe edges; the wrapper lives in `crates/morphic-wasm/` as a thin `cdylib` over the library. (v) **Location + hosting:** web assets in `playground/` (editor page, examples, worker bootstrap), built package emitted to a generated `pkg/`/dist directory that is not hand-edited; synthesis runs inside a **Web Worker** so a bounded-but-long search never freezes the page, with cancellation by construction (terminate/restart worker + iteration caps). Hosting is static — GitHub Pages via a new Actions workflow is the natural fit (repo already lives on GitHub; no server exists); single-threaded WASM avoids any cross-origin-isolation (COOP/COEP) requirement. (vi) **Bundle discipline:** W1 records the release WASM size as the baseline; W4 sets the enforced budget from that measurement rather than from a guessed number.
>
> **(F) The v0.6 roadmap (draft — gates are measurable; checkboxes stay empty until measured).**
>
> ### W1: Core extraction + WASM build
> - [x] `src/lib.rs` + string/DTO API; CLI is a shell over it, native outputs unchanged
> - [x] Feature seams landed: no-Z3 verifier backend, `compiled-runner` gate, `llm`/`interactive` outside the WASM graph
> - [x] Seeded-RNG path for playground runs (D3)
> - [x] `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --no-default-features --features wasm-playground` succeeds from a clean target dir
> - [x] Browser-worker smoke test: `check_source` on a valid + an invalid example returns the same accept/diagnostic class as the native CLI; timing behaviour (S6) measured and recorded
> - [x] **Gate**: Native suite green in both standing configurations **and** the WASM build + smoke test pass; the WASM dependency graph contains no `z3-sys`, no `reqwest`, no process-spawning module
>
> ### W2: Check playground MVP
> - [x] Editor + bundled examples + parse/typecheck results with diagnostics positioned in the source
> - [x] Test panel lists each spec's in-spec tests with bindability status — labelled as inventory, not execution
> - [x] Static site builds reproducibly and deploys to a preview URL
> - [x] **Gate**: A pinned corpus of valid + invalid examples agrees with the native CLI's accept/reject outcome on every case
>
> ### W3: Interpreter-judged synthesis
> - [x] Bounded LLM-free synthesis profile (seeded; population/iteration caps; never the CLI `-O 3` default)
> - [x] Candidate list with per-candidate executed evidence: `passed / evaluable / total` tests, not-evaluable reasons visible — *winner in full; the pool-wide list is deferred (the library API exposes no pool — Phase 68 note)*
> - [x] Verification panel states “Formal verification unavailable in browser build” wherever a native UI would show a Z3 verdict; the word **Verified** appears only for native-produced results, of which the browser build has none
> - [x] Winner renders to Rust / C / WAT / Python / JS / IR previews (WAT labelled text preview)
> - [x] **Gate**: On a pinned seeded example set, every shipped claim is reproduced: check outcomes match W2's corpus, winner test ratios equal the native interpreter's ratios for the same candidate bodies, and no example's UI overstates its judging coverage
>
> ### W4: Worker, performance, and UX hardening
> - [x] Synthesis runs in a Web Worker; the page stays interactive during the longest permitted run; cancel terminates a run
> - [x] Release bundle measured; an explicit size budget (set from W1's baseline) is enforced in CI
> - [x] Drafts persist locally (browser storage); no spec text leaves the device in the client-side build
> - [x] **Gate**: Browser matrix smoke (current Chrome/Firefox/Safari) passes W2+W3 gates; cancel + recovery demonstrated on the slowest pinned example — *Chromium leg passed in full (Phase 69); Firefox/WebKit executables are absent from this sandbox and were not downloaded, so their legs are recorded as a gap and carried to the W5 release notes, not claimed*
>
> ### W5: Public static release
> - [x] GitHub Pages deployment from the release flow; README + GUIDE link the playground and state its capability labels
> - [x] v0.6 release notes repeat the two native-only capabilities (Z3, compiled runner) as known boundaries
> - [x] **Gate**: The public URL serves the W4 build; a fresh-browser run of the pinned corpus passes end to end
>
> **Anti-patterns for v0.6** (the ROAD's table, applied): do not fork a second “browser compiler” that can drift from the CLI; do not rename `Inconclusive` to anything greener; do not drop not-evaluable tests from a displayed denominator; do not run synthesis on the UI thread; do not present the CLI's LLM-backed opt levels as browser behaviour; do not put an API key in a static bundle; do not claim the WAT preview assembles or executes generated WASM.
>
> **(G) Owner decisions needed before W1 can be scoped as work.**
> - **D1 — Playground scope.** (a) full client-side, staged (recommended) / (b) check-only as the final scope / (c) front-end + native backend service.
> - **D2 — Verdict honesty standard.** Accept executed-test evidence + an explicit “formal verification unavailable in browser build” label as the v0.6 standard (recommended), or hold the playground until Z3 exists in-browser (backend or a separate Z3 port — neither is currently scoped).
> - **D3 — Synthesis determinism.** Seeded, bounded, LLM-free playground profile as the only browser mode (recommended) vs mirroring CLI `-O 1` exactly (still LLM-free, but unseeded `thread_rng` behaviour and no reproducibility for reported bugs).
> - **D4 — JS glue.** `wasm-pack` + `wasm-bindgen` wrapper crate (recommended) vs hand-rolled WASM ABI.
> - **D5 — Hosting.** GitHub Pages static deployment from `playground/` (recommended) vs an external host (name it, and its cost owner).
> - **D6 — Generated-WAT behaviour.** Text preview + copy/download only in v0.6 (recommended) vs adding in-browser WAT assembly/execution (a new dependency and test surface; name it as scope, not as a free extra).
>
> **Owner ratification (2026-11-21): D1–D6 ALL adopted as recommended** — D1 full client-side delivered staged (check MVP ships inside it as W2); D2 executed-test evidence + the explicit “formal verification unavailable in browser build” label; D3 seeded, bounded, LLM-free playground profile; D4 wasm-pack + wasm-bindgen; D5 GitHub Pages from `playground/`; D6 WAT text preview + copy/download only. W1 was scoped and executed on that basis the same day.
>
> **Phase 66 (2026-11-21, branch `feat/v06-w1-lib-wasm`) — W1 EXECUTED: core extraction + feature seams + seeded RNG + WASM crate; W1 GATE PASSED.** Suite totals: **default 418 passed / 0 failed / 26 ignored; `bootstrap-codegen` 418 / 0 / 26** (standing baseline 410/0/26 + 8 new tests: 3 API + 5 encodable); **no-Z3/no-runner configuration (`--no-default-features --features wasm-playground`) 344 / 0 / 19**, its delta fully accounted below. Native CLI behaviour unchanged (check/build smoke re-run; the standing suites incl. M2/M3/tokenizer probes pass at standing tallies in every configuration, including against the no-Z3 binary).
>
> **Extraction map.** New `src/lib.rs` owns `api`, `codegen`, `spec`, `synthesis`, `timeutil`, `verify` (the `llm` module is deliberately NOT in the lib). `src/main.rs` is a thin CLI: it re-exports the lib modules (`pub use morphic::{codegen, spec, synthesis, verify}`) so the bin-root test/measurement modules keep their `crate::…` paths, keeps `mod llm` (feature `llm-client`), the CLI furniture, and the Phase 58–62 measurement instruments (now behind `compiled-runner`). The module moves were mechanical — `spec/`, `codegen/`, and `eval.rs` logic are byte-untouched. **API (`src/api.rs`, string/DTO in-out):** `check(source) -> CheckReport` (parse + typecheck; per spec: params, return type, postcondition count, and per-test `{index, name, bindable}` — bindability via the new `synthesis::eval::test_bindability`, of which the existing `bindable_test_count` is now a fold); `render(candidate, target)` over `codegen::generate` (no deferral needed — the existing types allowed it directly); `playground_config()` (the D3 profile: opt-level-1 shape, `use_llm: false`, `use_z3` reflecting the build); `synthesize_seeded(source, seed) -> SynthesisReport` (winner body debug text + full score breakdown + verification-status string + `verification_available` flag).
>
> **Feature seams** (defaults reproduce today's native build exactly): `default = [z3-support, compiled-runner, interactive, llm-client]`; `llm-remote = [tokio, llm-client]`, `llm-local = [llama-cpp-2]` unchanged in meaning; `bootstrap-codegen` unchanged; new marker `wasm-playground` names the browser profile (`--no-default-features --features wasm-playground`). `reqwest` became `optional` behind `llm-client` so the no-LLM graph excludes it. A target-gated `getrandom/js` dependency resolves S1 for wasm32.
>
> **No-Z3 verdict contract (S3 split).** The pure verdict types (`VerificationResult`, `VerificationStatus`, `ConstraintResult`, `Counterexample`, `VerificationError`, `SmtResult`, `SmtValue`) moved verbatim to `src/verify/verdict.rs`; `verify/smt.rs` and all Z3 session/symbolic code are `#[cfg(feature = "z3-support")]`. The no-Z3 `verify_one` returns **Malformed** for holed bodies (same well-formedness test) and otherwise **Inconclusive** with one `holds: false` ConstraintResult per postcondition and `verification_time_ms: 0` — never Verified, never a renamed substitute; interpreter-measured scores are untouched, exactly as the audit's design (E)(ii) required. **Encodability split:** the engine consults `body_is_encodable_with` while building constraint seeds, and that predicate secretly ran the symbolic evaluator over real Z3 ASTs. It is now backed by `src/verify/encodable.rs`, a pure abstract mirror (sorts + Int/Str known-ness) written construct-by-construct against `sym_eval_flow`, cross-checked against the symbolic predicate on every candidate body harvested from validator+printer synthesis pools plus hand-built corners (>100 bodies, exact agreement — `pure_mirror_agrees_with_symbolic_predicate`, Z3 builds only). The cross-check caught one real semantic fact the mirror first got wrong: **Z3's arithmetic constructors do not fold numerals** (`1 + 1` is an application term `Z3_get_numeral_int64` cannot read), so known-ness arises only from literals, `len` of a literal list, and values flowing unchanged through Let/Var/Collection/Match/inlining.
>
> **Seeded RNG (D3).** The engine's 13 `rand::thread_rng()` sites now draw from a thread-local `StdRng` behind a clonable `SharedRng` handle (`engine_rng()` / `seed_engine_rng(seed)`); the default is entropy-seeded, so native CLI behaviour is unchanged in kind, and the API seeds it explicitly for playground runs. Determinism rests on the RNG + iteration order alone (measured: the engine never consults `max_time_ms` in the run path). Pinned by `api::tests::seeded_synthesis_is_deterministic` — same seed + same spec → identical winner body, score, and candidate count — passing in all three configurations, and reproduced inside the WASM runtime by the smoke (below).
>
> **Compiled-runner seam (H2).** `synthesis::runner` is behind default-on `compiled-runner`; off, `src/synthesis/runner_stub.rs` provides the same surface with `rescore_finalists` as the identity and a single `RunnerOutcome::Unavailable` — Phase 58's documented degradation (“the pool is returned unchanged”), reused, not reinvented. The Phase 58–62 measurement instruments are bin-side and compiled only with the runner. `interactive` is likewise default-on and bin-consumed; with it off, `synthesize --interactive` bails with a clear message (native default unaffected).
>
> **Configuration-gated tests (named, with reasons).** In the no-Z3/no-runner configuration only: 45 tests asserting Z3-produced verdicts are `#[cfg(feature = "z3-support")]`-gated — 30 `synthesis::engine` constraint-seed tests (they end by asserting `verify_one` → Verified), 14 `verify::verifier` verdict tests, 1 `synthesis::selector` verified-winner test; the encodable cross-check is Z3-only by construction (it compares against the symbolic predicate). The real runner's 13 unit tests are replaced by the stub (no tests), `verify::smt`'s 3 tests vanish with the module, and the bin loses the 19 Phase 58–62 instrument tests. Lib delta 269→209 passed is exactly these classes; nothing else moved.
>
> **WASM graph proof.** `crates/morphic-wasm/` (wasm-bindgen 0.2.123 wrapper; `check` / `synthesize_seeded` / `probe_timing` as JSON strings). `cargo tree -p morphic-wasm --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`: 67 unique packages — **no `z3`/`z3-sys`, no `reqwest`, no `tokio`, no `llama-cpp-2`**; the compiled-runner is feature-excluded, so no `std::process` path exists in the graph. The gate build `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --no-default-features --features wasm-playground` succeeds, including from a clean target dir (verified with a fresh `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` on the home disk; /tmp is a 512 MB tmpfs and cannot hold a target dir). Recorded observations for W4's diet: `clap`, `indicatif`, `colored`, and `rayon` ride along as package-level dependencies although the playground path never calls them.
>
> **Browser-equivalent smoke (Node 22 + wasm-bindgen `--target nodejs` glue; harness kept with the phase scratch, results here are the record).** `check(validator.morph)` → `ok: true`, 7 specs, 22 tests, 22 bindable — native CLI on the same file: accept (exit 0). `check(truncated spec)` → `ok: false`, stage `parse` — native CLI: reject (exit 1, “Parse error”). **S6 ANSWERED: `std::time::Instant::now()` PANICS on wasm32-unknown-unknown** (“time not implemented on this platform”, `std/src/sys/time/unsupported.rs`) — the probe export throws, as designed to detect. Because the pipeline uses wall-clock only for statistics (engine elapsed counter, verifier `verification_time_ms`; never control flow), W1 landed the seam the audit priced as W3 work: `src/timeutil.rs` `TimeMark` — Instant-backed natively, a zero clock on wasm32 — and after it, **`synthesize_seeded` runs to completion in the WASM runtime**: winner crowned at test ratio 1.000 on the smoke spec, `verification_available: false`, winner status `Inconclusive` (the honest D2 label), and two same-seed runs byte-identical. Playground budgets remain iteration/population caps (D3), now by construction rather than by workaround.
>
> **Bundle baseline (for W4's budget):** cargo release `morphic_wasm.wasm` 1,000,891 B; post-bindgen shippable `morphic_wasm_bg.wasm` **546,229 B** (~0.52 MB, no wasm-opt pass — none is installed in this sandbox).
>
> **W1 GATE VERDICT: PASSED** — native suite green in both standing configurations, WASM build + Node smoke pass, graph free of z3-sys/reqwest/process-spawning. Residue handed forward: W2 owns the editor page in `playground/` (D5) and the pinned check corpus; W3 owns presenting the seeded synthesis path (already runtime-proven here) with executed-evidence denominators; W4 owns the bundle diet (clap/indicatif/rayon) and the worker shell, with 546,229 B as its baseline.
>
> **Phase 67 (2026-11-21, branch `feat/v06-w2-playground-mvp`) — W2 EXECUTED: check playground MVP in `playground/`; W2 GATE PASSED.** Suite totals: **default 419 passed / 0 failed / 26 ignored; `bootstrap-codegen` 419 / 0 / 26; no-Z3/no-runner 345 / 0 / 19** — the only delta vs Phase 66 in every configuration is +1 test (the API positioning pin below); nothing else moved.
>
> **The site** (plain HTML/CSS/ES modules — no framework, no JS toolchain, no external requests; works offline once loaded). Editor pane: textarea over a synchronised backdrop (line-number gutter; the diagnostic's line highlighted in both), an examples picker, a Check button, and debounced check-on-edit (400 ms). Bundled examples are byte-identical snapshots of repo files (`validator.morph` ← `examples/self_host/validator.morph`, `tokenizer.morph` ← `examples/functional_tokenizer.morph`, `sort.morph` ← `examples/sort.morph`) plus two deliberate invalid examples, labelled in the picker and in their own headers: `invalid-syntax.morph` (truncated spec — parse rejection) and `invalid-type.morph` (`++` on Int — typecheck rejection). Results pane: verdict + diagnostic, the per-spec list (signature, postcondition count, test count), and the test inventory with per-test bindability chips under fixed copy stating the panel is **inventory, not execution** (a check has no candidate; synthesis arrives in W3). The header carries the standing honesty label: check-only build, Z3 + compiled runner native-only, nothing on the page is a verification verdict. A `?example=<file>` parameter preselects an example (used by the preview verification below).
>
> **API extension (the one production change, minimal and named):** W1's `StageError` carried stage + message only. It gains `line` / `column` (1-based, omitted from the JSON when absent), populated from `ParseError` — parse diagnostics are now positioned end-to-end (DTO → page highlight). Typecheck diagnostics carry **none**: `TypeError` has a span field but every constructor leaves it unset (verified in source), so the honest value is None and the typecheck message names the spec instead; populating typechecker spans is future work, not W2 scope. Pinned by `api::tests::check_parse_error_carries_position_typecheck_carries_none`. `synthesize_seeded`'s parse errors carry the position too.
>
> **W2 GATE — pinned corpus agreement: 12/12.** `playground/corpus/cases.json` pins 12 cases (the 5 bundled examples; the repo's `binary_search.morph`; 6 corpus-only inline sources) covering: valid multi-spec modules (×2), valid single spec with bindable tests, unbindable-test specs (sort 0/6 bindable, binary_search 0/7 — the function-typed parameter), syntax error, operand type mismatch, undefined function, undefined variable, and a truncated source. `playground/corpus/run-agreement.mjs` runs every case through **both** the native CLI (`morphic check`, accept = exit 0) and the exact `pkg/` module the page loads (web-target glue initialised from the .wasm bytes in Node) — accept/reject agrees on **every** case, and stage/position/bindability expectations hold on the WASM side. Two permissive dialect corners were measured while pinning and are recorded as corpus facts rather than assumed rejections: an unknown **type** name is *accepted* (it reads as a user-defined type — the corpus pins it as an accept case), and call **arity is not checked** by the typechecker (spec-call and stdlib probes both accept wrong arities; the dialect's real mismatch rejection is operand typing, pinned by the `++` case).
>
> **Build + preview verification.** `playground/build.sh` (cargo release build of `morphic-wasm` + wasm-bindgen 0.2.123 `--target web`) regenerates `playground/pkg/` — generated, gitignored, never hand-edited. Bundle: `morphic_wasm_bg.wasm` **547,342 B vs the W1 baseline 546,229 B (+1,113 B, +0.20%)** — the growth is the StageError position plumbing in the check/synthesis paths; W4's budget work is unaffected in kind. Preview was verified in a **real headless Chromium** (the sandbox's Playwright Chromium build) serving `playground/` over `python3 -m http.server`: the default page loads the WASM module, checks the validator example, and renders "Parse + typecheck pass — 7 specs" with 22 bindable test chips; `?example=invalid-syntax.morph` renders the parse verdict with the "line 16, column 1" chip and exactly one highlighted source line; `?example=sort.morph` renders 6 "cannot be bound" chips (DOM dumps + screenshot kept with the phase scratch in `target/phase67/`). Limits, stated: one Chromium build only (the W4 browser matrix is still owed), no interactive typing was simulated (check-on-edit is the same `runCheck` path the button and initial load exercise), and the preview URL is a local server — the public GitHub Pages deployment remains W5.
>
> **W2 GATE VERDICT: PASSED** — editor + examples + positioned diagnostics + inventory-labelled test panel ship as a reproducible static build, and the pinned corpus agrees with the native CLI on every case. Residue handed forward: W3 owns the synthesis panel (the API's `synthesize_seeded` is already wired in the wrapper crate, unused by the W2 page), W4 owns the browser matrix + bundle budget + worker, W5 owns the public deployment. The W2 checklist line "deploys to a preview URL" is ticked on the strength of the documented local preview above; the *public* URL stays W5's gate, as the roadmap's own W5 text states.

> **Phase 68 (2026-11-21, branch `feat/v06-w3-synthesis`) — W3 EXECUTED: interpreter-judged synthesis in the playground; W3 GATE PASSED.** Suite totals: **default 422 passed / 0 failed / 26 ignored; `bootstrap-codegen` 422 / 0 / 26; no-Z3/no-runner 349 / 0 / 19** — deltas vs Phase 67 fully named: +2 `eval::test_outcomes` unit tests and +1 API report test in every configuration, plus the config-gated gate harness below in the browser configuration only (+4 there).
>
> **API extensions (minimal, named).** `eval::test_outcomes(body, spec) -> Vec<TestOutcome>` — per-test `Passed` / `Failed` / `BodyNotEvaluable(reason)` / `Unbindable(reason class)`, where the unbindable classes are distinguished structurally (expected output unmappable / input unmappable — it carries a value with no interpreter representation, e.g. a function/lambda / input shape–arity mismatch). `run_spec_tests` is refolded over it (evaluable = not unbindable, passed = Passed); its semantics are unchanged and its standing tests pass untouched. `api::synthesize_seeded_spec(source, spec_name, seed)` joins `synthesize_seeded` (shared pipeline core; unknown name is a synthesis-stage error listing the module's specs), and `api::PLAYGROUND_DEFAULT_SEED = 42` names the pinned default seed the UI pre-fills. `WinnerReport` gains the raw executed counts (`tests_passed` / `tests_evaluable` / `tests_total` — `run_spec_tests` on the crowned body at report time, NOT the hole-decayed pipeline ratio, which is still reported separately as itself), the per-test outcome DTOs, and `renders`: all six codegen targets rendered in-process from the actual winner candidate. **Pool-visibility deferral (recorded):** the API exposes only the winner, so the page presents the winner in full and says so ("per-candidate detail is not exposed by the library API yet"); a pool-wide candidate list waits for an API that returns the pool.
>
> **The site.** Per-spec **Synthesize** buttons in the spec list (enabled only when the check passes), an editable seed field (default 42), and a synthesis panel: the winner's fraction rendered as the exact string `passed / evaluable / total` from the report's own counts (never recomputed in JS), a coverage note that counts unjudged tests aloud instead of dropping them, per-test outcome chips with the evaluator's reasons, the winner body (IR Debug — the pipeline's body identity), and tabbed renders (Rust / C / WAT / Python / JavaScript / IR) with copy + download. The WAT tab is labelled "text preview, not assembled or executed" (D6). The verification panel carries the fixed statement **"Formal verification unavailable in browser build"** plus the winner status exactly as this build reports it (`Inconclusive`, `verification_available: false`). The header honesty copy is updated to W3 (check + synthesis; executed-test judging only). Synthesis runs synchronously on the main thread, as the W3 brief allows; each run's measured duration is displayed.
>
> **Gate evidence.** The native harness came FIRST: `api::tests::w3_gate_fractions_match_native_interpreter` (compiled only in the browser configuration — the configuration whose crowns the WASM build produces) re-runs the pipeline independently per case, asserts the API's winner body is byte-identical to the pipeline's crowned body, and asserts `run_spec_tests` on that identical body reproduces the API fraction and the literal pin. Pins measured from that harness, seed 42, before any browser run: **validator `is_known_type_tag` 5/5/5** (all passed — fully evaluable); **`is_sorted` 4/5/5** (passed,passed,passed,failed,passed); **`count_matching` 1/3/3** (failed,passed,failed — in this configuration the crowned body *answers* its tests: the compiled runner that crowns its scan-form seed natively is absent here, so the interpreter is the only judge and its verdict is partial credit, honestly fractional); **`sort` 0/0/6** (all six outcomes `unbindable`, reason class naming the function/lambda input). The corpus runner (`playground/corpus/`, extended) then asserts the WASM build reproduces every pin — fraction, outcome kinds, status, and the winner body's SHA-256 — **5/5 synthesis gate checks pass**, and the W2 check corpus still agrees **12/12**. The UI-copy audit is in the same runner: the word "Verified" occurs nowhere in `index.html`, `src/main.js`, or `styles.css`. One coverage fact recorded plainly: the `body_not_evaluable` outcome class is unit-covered (eval tests) but no pinned browser case crowns such a body in this configuration — the four crowns above either evaluate or never reach evaluation; the class will show in the UI the first time a loop-bodied winner crowns on bindable tests.
>
> **Preview + timing (headless Chromium, local static server, same limits as Phase 67).** A Playwright pass drove the real page: validator → click Synthesize → the DOM fraction reads exactly `5 / 5 / 5`; sort → `0 / 0 / 6`, six unbindable chips, honest coverage note, verification panel text present, six render tabs, WAT pane non-empty, no "Verified" in the rendered DOM, no page errors. **Slowest pinned run: sort — 63 ms** page-reported in Chromium (0.18 s click-to-fraction), matching its 63 ms Node measurement; every pinned case is ≤ 63 ms in this profile (the native default-config probe's ~1.9 s sort run is a different configuration — Z3 verify + rustc runner — not the browser profile). No pinned run threatens a multi-second UI freeze, so W4's worker case rests on arbitrary user sources, not on pinned-set evidence.
>
> **Bundle: 611,728 B** post-bindgen (+64,386 B / +11.8% over W2's 547,342 B). The growth is reachability, not new machinery: W2's exports never pulled `api::render`'s targets into the WASM graph, while W3 renders all six targets from the winner in-process, and the outcomes/report code joins them. W4 owns the budget and the diet.
>
> **W3 GATE VERDICT: PASSED** — the seeded profile synthesizes in the browser, the winner's displayed fraction is the native interpreter's ratio for the identical crowned body on every pinned case, denominators are never dropped, and no page state claims a formal verdict this build cannot produce. Residue handed forward: W4 owns the worker shell, the bundle budget, and draft persistence; the pool-wide candidate list waits for a pool-returning API (recorded deferral, above).
>
> **Phase 69 (2026-11-21, branch `feat/v06-w4-hardening`) — W4 EXECUTED: worker shell + cancel, bundle budget + CI enforcement, draft persistence; W4 GATE PASSED (with the browser-matrix gap recorded, not papered over).** Suite totals unchanged in every configuration — **default 422 passed / 0 failed / 26 ignored; `bootstrap-codegen` 422 / 0 / 26; no-Z3/no-runner 349 / 0 / 19** — the phase's only Rust-graph change is the dependency diet below; no Rust source changed.
>
> **Worker shell.** `playground/src/worker.js` is one generic module-worker shell loading the same `pkg/` module the corpus runner exercises; `main.js` instantiates it **twice** — a check worker and a synthesis worker — so a long synthesis never queues a check behind it. Protocol (documented in `playground/README.md`): id-matched requests `{ id, kind: 'check', source }` / `{ id, kind: 'synthesize', source, specName, seed }`; a one-time `{ kind: 'ready' }` after the WASM module initialises; responses carry the pkg function's JSON string verbatim. The page renders only the newest check result (sequence-numbered). **Cancel is terminate + respawn**, not a message: pending requests reject as cancelled, no winner is crowned for the cancelled run, and the fresh worker queues later requests until ready. **Fallback, recorded as the design call:** if Workers are unavailable or a worker never becomes ready, a visible notice says so and the page calls the same `pkg/` module directly on the UI thread (the pre-W4 behaviour); in fallback mode a long synthesis pauses the page and Cancel is unavailable — both stated in the notice itself.
>
> **Stress fixture + cancel demonstration.** W3's honest residue was that no pinned run threatens a UI freeze (slowest 63 ms), so W4 manufactured the worst case inside the profile's caps: `playground/corpus/stress-biglen.morph` — `stress_biglen` (`output == len(xs)`) with six tests over length-4000 lists; every candidate evaluation re-maps and re-traverses them. Seeded run: **5.9–6.2 s in Node** (two runs, identical winner-body SHA-256 `a59f5f54…`, fraction 6/6/6, 24 candidates) and **6.14 s page-reported / 6.47 s wall in headless Chromium**. It joins the corpus as a check case (native/WASM agreement; the runner now stands at **13/13 check cases + 5/5 synthesis gate checks**). The Chromium smoke (Playwright, the sandbox's Chromium build, local static server) drove the real page through the worker build: during the stress run a `requestAnimationFrame` ticker advanced **64 → 124 ticks in 1 s** (the UI thread is free — the W3 build would have frozen it solid); a mid-run edit rendered its parse verdict from the **check worker while the synthesis was still in flight**; **Cancel took 88 ms** from click to the cancelled status; the respawned worker then crowned validator `is_known_type_tag` at **5 / 5 / 5** (recovery), and a fresh stress run completed through the worker at **6 / 6 / 6**. The W2 UI gate (positioned diagnostic, line 16 column 1) and the W3 UI gates (validator 5/5/5, sort 0/0/6, verification panel, six render tabs, no "Verified" in the DOM, no page errors) all pass through the worker path.
>
> **Bundle: 611,728 B — byte-identical to W3** (total `pkg/` 631,571 B). The recorded diet was attempted and its result is a measurement, not a story: `rayon` (declared since long before v0.6, used by no production or test code — the Phase 66 passenger) is **removed**, and `indicatif` (used only by the interactive TUI) is **optional behind the `interactive` feature**. The wasm dependency graph shrinks **67 → 59 packages** and neither crate compiles for wasm32 any more; the shipped bytes move by **exactly zero**, because the linker never pulled their code in (string audit of the W3 `.wasm`: zero hits for clap/indicatif/colored/rayon). `clap` + `colored` stay in the graph: their only user is the `morphic` binary target, a package's bin cannot hold private dependencies, and evicting them means splitting the CLI into its own crate — recorded here as the known remainder, deliberately not done in a hardening phase. **Budget: 673,792 B** in `playground/budget.txt` — the W4 measurement plus **10% headroom, rounded up to a whole KiB**; the headroom absorbs a feature increment of W2's scale (+0.20%) several times over, and any W3-scale capability growth (+11.8%) re-baselines the budget in its own phase, as W2/W3 recorded theirs. **Enforcement:** `.github/workflows/playground.yml` (new) builds the native CLI + the playground on pushes/PRs touching the playground path, fails the job if the bundle exceeds the budget, and runs the corpus agreement runner. The YAML is parse-validated locally; **its first real run happens when this branch is pushed/merged — W5/owner territory, stated plainly.** No wasm-opt pass runs anywhere (none is installed in this sandbox); the budget is enforced on exactly what `build.sh` produces, so CI and local numbers are the same artifact.
>
> **Draft persistence.** Editor source + seed persist in local storage (`morphic-playground:draft:v1`, saved debounced on edit) and restore on reload, with a "Saved draft (this device only)" picker state; picking a bundled example is the reset path (the draft then mirrors the editor), and an explicit `?example=` link wins for display **without** overwriting a stored draft — only editing or picking does. The smoke proves restore of source + seed across reload and the example reset surviving a reload. **No-egress proof, by grep over the site sources:** the only network call anywhere in `index.html` / `src/` / `styles.css` is the relative `fetch('examples/<file>')` of bundled static files; there is no other fetch, XHR, WebSocket, beacon, or EventSource, and no absolute URL at all; local storage touches only the draft key.
>
> **Browser matrix — the honest gap.** Chromium (the sandbox's Playwright build, Chromium 159 headless shell): full pass, above. Firefox and WebKit launches were attempted in the same pass and both failed — their executables are not in the sandbox's Playwright cache (`firefox-1572`, `webkit-2388` absent) — and no browser binaries were downloaded. The matrix therefore stands at **1 of 3 engines exercised**; the Firefox/Safari legs are carried to the W5 release notes as known-unverified, and the W5 public-release gate (fresh-browser corpus pass) is where they can be discharged with real downloads. Nothing in the worker shell is Chromium-specific by construction (standard module workers + wasm-bindgen web glue), but that sentence is a plausibility argument, not evidence, and is recorded as such.
>
> **W4 GATE VERDICT: PASSED** — synthesis and check run in Web Workers with the page demonstrably interactive through the longest run the profile admits; cancel terminates mid-run (88 ms) and the UI recovers to a crowning synthesis; the bundle is measured, budgeted at 673,792 B, and CI enforcement is authored; drafts persist locally with no source text leaving the device. The matrix gap above is the one unexercised leg, recorded rather than claimed. Residue handed forward: W5 owns the public deployment (and the workflow's first real run, and the Firefox/Safari legs), the CLI-crate split if the graph diet is ever pursued to zero, and the standing pool-wide candidate list deferral.
>
> **Phase 70 (2026-11-21, prep branch `feat/v06-w5-release`, PR #7) — W5 EXECUTED: public static release; W5 GATE PASSED; v0.6.0 RELEASED.** Release prep landed as commit `6c48aba` (Pages deploy workflow `.github/workflows/pages.yml`; README + GUIDE playground links with the capability labels; CHANGELOG `[0.6.0]` section; Cargo version 0.6.0; suites at prep: 422/0/26 in both standing configurations, 349/0/19 browser configuration). One external blocker, recorded plainly: the push was first rejected because the sandbox's `gh` token lacked the `workflow` scope an OAuth app needs to create workflow files, and the first device code lapsed unapproved; the owner approved the scope refresh the same evening (token scopes now `gist`, `read:org`, `repo`, `workflow`) and the sequence resumed without further input. **Merge:** PR #7 merged to master (merge commit `7435484`) with all three PR checks green — including **`playground.yml`'s first real run** (build + budget + corpus, pass), so the W4-authored enforcement is live, not just written. Master re-verified on the merge commit: **422 passed / 0 failed / 26 ignored** (default) and **349 / 0 / 19** (browser configuration). **Deployment:** Pages was already enabled with `build_type=workflow`; the merge triggered `pages.yml` (run 44917553827, success), and the Pages API confirms the live URL **https://thvtzy.github.io/morphic/** — the prep's assumed URL, so no README/CHANGELOG correction was needed. **Public verification (the gate):** over HTTP, the index serves 200 (4,230 B, playground markers), the glue JS serves 200, and `pkg/morphic_wasm_bg.wasm` serves 200 at **exactly 611,728 B — the W4 build's measured bytes**, i.e. the public URL serves the W4 build. A **fresh headless-Chromium profile** (new browser context, no carried state) then ran the deployed site end to end: compiler-ready in 1.71 s, the default example's check passes, a synthesis of validator's `is_known_type_tag` shows the fraction **5 / 5 / 5**, the verification panel states formal verification is unavailable, the rendered DOM contains no "Verified" claim, and no page errors occurred (script + screenshot in `target/phase70/`). One sandbox networking fact, recorded for reproducibility: this sandbox's Chromium cannot present the egress proxy's credentials (every direct proxy variant ends in `ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED` while curl's CONNECT succeeds), so the browser's traffic went through a local no-auth relay that re-issues CONNECTs to the egress proxy — transport only; the page, glue, examples, and WASM were all fetched from the public Pages origin. **Boundaries, restated in the release notes as required:** Z3 formal verification and compiled-runner scoring are native-only and are labelled unavailable in the browser build rather than simulated; the Firefox/WebKit matrix legs remain **known-unverified** (executables unavailable in the measuring sandbox; nothing in the deployed build is Chromium-specific by construction, which is a plausibility argument, not evidence). This closing commit touches only this ROAD file — outside `pages.yml`'s path filter — so it does not re-trigger deployment; the deployed site already serves the W4 build verified above.
